# Status

> Short factual handoff for human reviewers and for restarting a Cline session.
> Update after every commit. Do not paste chat transcripts.
> Enforced: `scripts/check-status.ps1` runs in `verify-quick.ps1` and fails
> when any commit hash referenced here does not resolve, when a backticked
> repo path claimed here does not exist, or when a roadmap ID here is absent
> from `docs/03-ROADMAP.md`.

- **R4.7D is closed; the live Office gate ran and passed 46/46 (this change)** — The three gaps recorded below are now closed: managed row heights are normalised, outline groups are rebuilt from `ParentId`, and `verify-office.ps1` has run to exit 0. `R4.7A`, `R4.7B` and `R4.7D` are complete.

  **Row-height normalisation and outline rebuild are pure in Core, COM-only in Office.** `RowHeightNormaliser.Plan` turns measured heights into a write set and excludes rows already at the token, so a correctly normalised sheet produces an **empty** write set and a Refresh does not mark the workbook dirty. It **refuses** a non-positive target and an unrecognised `MeasuredRowKind` rather than defaulting either, because a wrong row height is precisely the misalignment the work removes. `OutlineGroupPlanner.Plan` derives groups from `ParentId` and **never reads the outline back**, so collapsing a group cannot change the model. Stale groups are cleared before new ones are written because Excel refuses to regroup a disagreeing sheet, and the two lists **never overlap** — a defect my own first draft had, where a stray child was both grouped and ungrouped, leaving the result dependent on write order. `OutlineGroup` also needed a hand-written `Equals`/`GetHashCode`: as a record holding a list it compared by reference, so two identical plans compared unequal and the order-independence test was unsatisfiable.

  **`Range.Group` is NOT the row-outlining API, and calling it would have silently done the wrong thing.** Reflection over the installed `Microsoft.Office.Interop.Excel` 14.0.1 assembly (from `exceldna.interop`) shows the only `Group` on `Range`/`IRange` is the **PivotTable** signature `(Start, End, By, Periods)`; `Range.Rows` is a plain `Range`, so the row-outlining overload is not reachable through this PIA. Calling it positionally would have sent an outline level as a PivotTable start value. The writer therefore sets the **metadata-verified** read/write `Range.OutlineLevel` (level 1 for a top-level row, 2 for a child) instead. This is the one place to revisit if a future interop package restores the real overload, and the reason is recorded at the call site.

  **The Critical Interval depth conflict is resolved by owner ruling (2026-09-29): two levels, one child and one parent.** `MaxDepth = 2`, the `EntityHierarchyCatalog` contract comment and `EntityProjection` all said a Critical Interval's own parent must be top-level; only `GanttRowValidator` exempted Critical Interval children from the depth check, and a passing test pinned that exemption as "pre-existing landed behaviour". The **exemption is withdrawn** — the depth rule is now uniform in the child's type, so a Critical Interval under an activity cannot own a child. The case the ruling leaves intact is covered: a **top-level** Critical Interval may still own a child (level 1 → 2), which is why `CriticalInterval` remains among the types that may own children. Both sides are pinned by new positive tests. A projection test's doc comment had been claiming the inner interval "still resolves" while its body asserted a refusal; that contradiction is gone.

  **Running the live gate for the first time found 5 pre-existing failures that no other gate could see.** All were stale literals in `Office.IntegrationTests` left behind by R4.7A's `SiblingOrder` column: two hard-coded column counts (`14`, now 15), two hard-coded plot-anchor column letters (`$O$1`, now `$P$1`), and — the serious one — `AssertRow` reading `StyleKey` at literal index 9, which after the insertion was reading `SiblingOrder`'s blank cell. **That helper had been passing for the wrong reason**: it compared the expected style key against `null` and the assertion was never exercised against a real value. All five now derive from `GanttTableSchema.Default.Columns` or read Excel's own address, and `AssertRow` cross-checks each name's implied index, so a future column insertion fails loudly instead of silently reading the wrong cell. This is the second time in R4.7 that a hard-coded positional assertion hid a schema shift, and it is the strongest argument yet for the name-resolved approach.

  **Verification** (all observed this change): Core **1151/1151** (was 1122), Office.ContractTests **526/526** (was 512), Architecture **84/84**, AddIn **203/203**, Raster **41/41**; `verify-quick.ps1` **PASS**; `verify-office.ps1` **PASS, 46/46, exit 0** (OfficeIntegration, 7m 15s); Release build 0 warnings with `/warnaserror`.

- **R4.7B is complete and R4.7D has both Core halves (this change)** — Four implementation commits since the documentation revision, and both human-reviewed golden regenerations approved. `R4.7A` is complete; `R4.7B` is complete; `R4.7D` has its Core half and is **not** closed.

  **R4.7B (`14b47f3`, `11130c3`)** resolves each entity's render lane in Core and wires it through. Three defects the wiring exposed. **A projected child was stacking and growing the lane**: grouping it into the parent's lane was not enough, because `LaneLayoutBuilder` still gave it a fresh index by position, growing the lane **8pt → 18pt** — exactly what ADR-0026 D7 forbids. A child now inherits its owner's index in two passes, so a child authored *above* its parent still resolves. **That rule then diverged in a second place**: `LaneEventLayout` re-derived the index independently, searched for a slot that no longer existed and refused the whole scene (33 failures). An earlier fix of mine counted owners across the whole batch rather than per lane and caused the *same* 33; it was reverted, not iterated on. **The overlap warning was reporting the product working**: a child overlapping its parent is the intended presentation per REV6 §8, yet it raised `AmbiguousStackOverlap`, which also collided with genuine ambiguity on the same owner. Now suppressed for projection pairs only.

  **The R4.7B golden** (human-reviewed, approved): 37 characters across 34 regions, primitives **85 → 85**, first difference `"Y":195 → "Y":175` with every later lane offset by the same 20pt — one lane sat 20pt lower because the critical interval no longer takes a slot of its own.

  **R4.7D (`9242a8a`, `3038927`)** removes lane auto-growth and then makes the lane height *measured* rather than chosen. Growth is **removed, not capped**; content that does not fit emits `LaneContentExceedsRowHeight` and is **neither compressed nor accommodated**. `LaneMetricsResolver` turns the measured per-row heights into lane metrics — no new Office adapter was needed, because the existing panel-grid port already reads each body row's height individually (ADR-0023's reason). The **most common** measured height wins, not the maximum and not the minimum: one dragged row must not resize every lane, and one compressed row must not shrink them all. A non-positive height is refused rather than substituted, because a default would silently misalign the chart from the worksheet.

  **The R4.7D golden** (human-reviewed, approved): **+606 bytes, primitives 58 → 58, and no geometry moved** — unlike R4.7B's, this one only *adds* three honest warnings for lanes that had been silently growing.

  **The same (owner, code) collision has now appeared three times** — in `LaneEventLayout`, in the new overflow warning, and in `SceneBuilder` concatenating two warning lists that each already carried the same warnings. `SceneValidator` keys duplicate warnings by (owner, code), so a rule producing a warning from more than one source, or for a row owning several lanes, reads as a defect. **The pattern is now recorded at each site**, because it is a property of the validator rather than of any one producer.

  **What R4.7D still needs, and why it is not closed.** Nothing yet *normalises* managed row heights to `GanttRowHeightPt`, outline groups are not yet rebuilt from `ParentId`, and `verify-office.ps1` has **not** been run. The central promise — a lane's Top/Height/Bottom equal its Excel row's exactly, across expand/collapse — is a claim about Excel that only a live host can confirm. The Core work makes growth impossible; it does not yet prove alignment.

  Commits: `0fb7ba8` `0fb006c` `f6fdd4b` `78cf1b9` `406e64a` (R4.7A) · `14b47f3` `11130c3` (R4.7B) · `9242a8a` `3038927` (R4.7D). Observed: Core **1122/1122**, Office.ContractTests **512/512**, `verify-quick` **PASS in 267.0s**, check-md-links OK across 191 files. `verify-office.ps1` **Not run** — every commit so far is Core-only or adds no COM. Checklist: A, B, C, E, I, M. **Next: wire the resolver into the Refresh path, add row-height normalisation and outline rebuilding, then run the live gate.**

- **The pre-R4.9 documentation revision is complete; no product code changed (previous change)** — Two input documents, `docs/GanttCreator_R3_R4_Architecture_Roadmap_Review_REV5.md` and `docs/GanttCreator_Revised_Implementation_Plan_REV6.md`, proposed the corrections the product needs before Refresh can be locked. They were **untracked**: an uncommitted `.gitignore` edit excluded both, so the roadmap would have cited requirements no reader could resolve. They are now tracked, and both carry a header naming the proposals that did not survive review. **The finding that mattered was that five of the input document's proposals did not survive, and an earlier draft of the plan implemented one of them wrongly.**

  **`docs/PRE-R4.9-DECISION-REGISTER.md` is the anti-drift artefact.** The resolved decisions: lane auto-growth is removed (reversing `LaneLayoutBuilder`'s `Math.Max(LaneHeightPt, contentHeight)` and the `08-TEST-CHECKLIST.md` §B invariant that states the same rule); the critical interval stays a **filled rectangle**; `Above`/`Below` label positions are removed; the existing unique shape key stands and REV5/REV6's `HostShapeKey` is rejected, which re-decides R4.7's L18 refusal the owner had already closed; and there is no migration work, because the owner confirmed there are no active users.

  **The critical decision was taken on evidence, and the evidence contradicted both the guide and my first reading of it.** The guide states that the critical interval is a rect in the scene but a **line** in every host renderer. Checking source: `SceneShapeRenderer` has no critical branch, the PowerPoint (Phase 7) and raster (Phase 8) renderers **do not exist**, and the committed golden contains **no critical overlay primitive** — its only `critical` occurrence is the `CriticalIntervalOutsideParent` warning, because the canonical fixture's critical interval lies wholly outside its parent. **Zero of three renderers implemented the guide's rule, and the golden never pinned the representation.** That is how the divergence survived. The owner's ruling — a rectangle is easier to size — resolves it in the one direction that needs no renderer cooperation: the rect becomes the host object, filled with a new `CriticalFill` token carrying `#FF0000`, at half the parent bar's resolved height. It also removes the need for the `SceneLine` change REV5 §7/§16 and REV6 §9/§16 proposed.

  **One reading of mine was wrong and is withdrawn in the register.** I first read the owner's label ruling as replacing ADR-0015's widest-gap truncation with a hard suppression, and proposed superseding ADR-0015 accordingly. The owner corrected it: there is already a function, it truncates when there is no space, and nothing new needs building. `LabelPlanner` does already implement the whole cascade — `TruncatedToFitCode` at line 130, `WasTruncated` at 65/71, `_spanAutoOrder` already beginning at `Right` at 139-141, and `FreeRight`/`FreeLeft` at 658/685. **ADR-0015 is therefore amended, not superseded.**

  **A latent gate defect was found and fixed before the roadmap could cite the new IDs.** `scripts/check-status.ps1` matched roadmap IDs with `\bR\d+\.\d+\b`, which **cannot match `R4.7A`**: `7` and `A` are both word characters, so no boundary exists between them. Every letter-suffixed row would have been invisible to the gate. **My first fix used a lower-case-only `[a-z]?` class and matched none of the UPPER-case suffixes; two tests written to assert the violation passed it silently.** That is why they are explicit positive tests: 31/31 pass.

  Roadmap revision 10 inserts nine rows, `R4.7A`–`R4.7H` and `R4.8A`, all completing before `R4.9`. The row count is **124**, counted from the phase tables; revision 9's stated 111 did not match the tables, and that discrepancy predates this revision, so it is recorded rather than absorbed. Entity guide revision 8 changes three sections — §9 lane geometry, §16 the critical rectangle, §22 label positions — and each **reverses something the guide previously stated**. Checklists L, M and N are new. **A third defect was found and corrected in `65dba1d`:** `R4.7A` and `R4.7C` both claimed the schema 3 → 4 bump, which would have collided or skipped a version in a way `ConfigIntegrity` cannot detect. Each schema-changing row now takes its own bump — R4.7A 3 → 4, R4.7C 4 → 5.

  Commits: `f14045f` `68411e8` `dc61f49` `03a9fd3` `1a6abde` `0753ec0`. `verify-office.ps1` **Not run** — documentation and one script only. Checklist: C, I.

- **R4.7A has started: the child-capability matrix and the `ParentId` widening are landed (this change)** — Two slices of `R4.7A` are in. The product owner chose the ordering question as **3 then 1**: introduce the child-capability matrix in R4.7A, then widen `ParentId` to every child-capable type. The matrix is `EntityHierarchyCatalog`, answering two questions per type — may it own children, may it be a child — with `MaxChildrenPerParent = 7` and `MaxDepth = 2` pinned by tests. **Two existing tests failed when the widening landed, and both were informative rather than incidental.** One encoded the superseded rule that a `ParentId` on an `As-Planned Activity` was a `NotUsedByType` warning; it is now a real child whose unresolvable parent blocks, and the test was replaced by four that assert the new contract positively.

  **The matrix was wrong on its first draft and a landed test caught it.** I classified `CriticalInterval` as child-only, reasoning that nesting is capped at two levels. That broke `Critical_interval_child_of_an_ambiguous_interval_is_blocked_in_either_input_order`, because **a Critical Interval can be the parent of another Critical Interval** and the child is commonly authored *above* its parent — pre-existing product behaviour, not something this sequence introduced. The matrix now classifies it as both, and my own test that asserted child-only was corrected with the reason recorded. Without the mutation check I would have shipped a hierarchy restriction the product never asked for.

  **A real defect was found in the widening itself, not just in the matrix.** The existing `else if (parentText is not null)` branch was reachable by any non-critical row, so a child-capable span carrying a real `ParentId` emitted a spurious **"not used by this Type" warning alongside its legitimate `ParentUnknown` error** — telling the user their `ParentId` was ignored and that their parent did not exist, at the same time. The block is now split three ways: a Critical Interval requires a parent; any other child-capable type may carry one, and **blank means top-level, which is what makes a promoted child valid again after its parent is deleted**; a type the matrix excludes still reports `NotUsedByType`. This is the D9 "nothing becomes silently permitted" clause caught in the other direction than it was written to catch.

  Both the matrix's exhaustiveness guard and the widened branch were **verified non-vacuous by mutation**: removing `AsBuiltMilestone` from the child set fails two tests independently, one naming the offending type; restricting the widened branch back to critical-only fails three. **A stale-artefact trap was also hit and worked around** — restoring a file with `Copy-Item` preserves its original timestamp, so MSBuild skipped recompiling and the suite ran against the mutated DLL while `git diff` showed a clean tree. This is the same trap already recorded in this file; the fix was an explicit `--no-incremental` build, and the green result after it is the one that counts.

  Commits: `0fb7ba8` the matrix · `0fb006c` the `ParentId` widening. Observed: Core **1043/1043** PASS (1038 before this row, 5 net new). `verify-quick.ps1` **PASS in 216.2s**; the preceding run's failure in `AddInHostTests.AutoOpen_...` was a **live Excel instance in the developer's Visual Studio session**, confirmed by the product owner — that test passes with these files present, absent, and in isolation. `verify-office.ps1` **Not run** — Core only, no COM, no renderer. Checklist: A, C, I, L, M. **Next: the remaining R4.7A slices** — `IsSemanticallyEmptyRow`, the seven-child cap, promote-on-delete, atomic clone reseed, and the `SiblingOrder` column with the schema 3 → 4 bump.

Each schema-changing row now takes its own bump — R4.7A 3 → 4, R4.7C 4 → 5.

- **The COM-proxy ratchet is blocked on determinism, not on the ceiling (this change)** — a review asked for `MaxForcedKills` to be brought down to the measured count once `OfficeFixture.SuppressLeakSignal` had been adopted. The suppressions **are** adopted, on all seven tests that force a kill on purpose. The ceiling was still not lowered, because the measurement does not support it.

  Three full 46-test runs of that tree measured **24, 26 and 26** (2026-09-28, Office `16.0.20326.20158` x64), matching the 24/25/26 spread already on record. A ceiling of 24 was set and the next run measured 26 and **failed the gate on a suite that was 46/46 green** — a gate that fails at random detects nothing. So 26 stands: the highest value observed, and the only defensible upper bound for a load-dependent signal. This is a refusal to make the requested change, made on evidence rather than caution.

  What did change is the reasoning around the number. The script comment still said adopting `SuppressLeakSignal` was "the next step" and that the ratchet's downward direction was not restored — both untrue now that the suppressions have landed. L19 said the same and named a remedy that has since been carried out without moving the measurement. Both now describe the suppressions as **landed**, the ceiling as **blocked on determinism**, and the real next step: per-test kill attribution in the forced-kill log, so the next person can see *which* teardown escalates rather than only how many did. That is what determinism needs before a ceiling can mean anything.

  Observed: `pwsh ./scripts/verify-office.ps1` → **`verify-office: PASS`, 46/46**, leak signal 26 against the ceiling of 26; `test-scripts.ps1` exit 0; `check-md-links` and `check-status` PASS. No product code changed. Checklist: C, J, K.

- **Three test defects: one that could not fail, one that reported a pass as a fail, and a duplicate (commit `794bc9d`)** — all three were found by checking the tests against the code they claim to cover, and two of them only became visible when the live Office gate was actually run.

  **`A_failing_technical_record_sink_never_escapes_the_dispose` was proving nothing.** It drove a bare `ExcelApplicationStateScope` over a `Mock<Excel.Application>`, whose `ScreenUpdating` reads as `false`; so `SuppressScreenUpdating` captured nothing, `Dispose` restored nothing, `Report` was never called, and the throwing sink was never invoked. The test would have passed with the catch in `Report` deleted. It now uses `TestableScope` with an injected `ScreenUpdating` restore failure — the only thing that reaches the sink — and asserts the sink was called **once**. That count is the load-bearing part: removing the injection fails the test, which is how the old vacuity was confirmed rather than assumed.

  **The live gate then exposed two defects in `e2a2ea4`, which was committed without ever being run.** `ApplicationStateScopeIntegrationTests` tracked the fixture's own workbook through its `ComScope`, so the same RCW was released twice: the scope's release separated it, and the fixture's teardown threw `InvalidComObjectException` out of `wb.Close`. `CreateWorkbook` documents that it hands the proxy to the fixture, which closes *and* releases it, so the test must not own it; the proxy is now deliberately left untracked with the reason in a comment. Separately, the test asserted `"C7"` where `Range.Address` returns `"$C$7"` — the cursor *had* moved, so this was a test defect reporting a product pass as a fail. The chained `application.ActiveSheet.Range["C7"]` is now a tracked `ActiveSheet` local plus a tracked range, and both `Selection` reads are tracked rather than left as unnamed proxies.

  `Null_bounds_are_refused` asserted the same refusal twice on the same input; the duplicate is gone.

  Observed: `pwsh ./scripts/verify-office.ps1` → **`verify-office: PASS`, 46/46**, Office `16.0.20326.20158` x64 — the first green run of this test since `e2a2ea4` (the run before it was 45/46 and is what exposed both). Office contract **503/503**; full non-Office suite **1850/1850**; `test-scripts.ps1` exit 0. Checklist: C, J, K.

- **The shape writer's update path contained host failures it used to let escape (commit `e8dc063`)** — a COM write the host refuses in `ApplyText` or `ApplyStyle` escaped `ExcelShapeWriter.Update` as an unhandled exception from inside a render command, where R4.7's reconciler only knows how to act on a typed result. `Create` already contained the same two writes; the update path did not. Both are now contained and report `HostRejected`.

  The guard is **not** a blanket catch. `MapHatchPattern` and `MapAlignment` raise `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` for a scene value with no host mapping — a contract fault between the entity guide and the adapter — and that still propagates, because a refusal would report "the host said no" for a defect this repository owns. Containment is also **not** a rollback: `ApplyGeometry` has already run, so a refused style leaves the shape moved and un-restyled. That asymmetry with `Create`, which *does* discard a shape it could not finish, is deliberate — deleting and recreating an owned shape to undo a momentary host refusal would discard the user's chart — and `IShapeWritePort.Update` said "on a refusal nothing was mutated", which was untrue. It now states the real behaviour and that callers must treat a refusal as "this shape is not yet correct", not "nothing happened".

  Three tests, each mutation-checked: removing the containment fails exactly the two refusal tests; removing the `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` arm fails exactly the contract-fault test. The refused-update test also pins the two things a reader would otherwise have to infer — the matched shape is **not** discarded, and its geometry really was moved.

  Observed: Release `-warnaserror` 0 warnings / 0 errors; Office contract **500 → 503/503**; full non-Office suite **1850/1850**; `dotnet format --verify-no-changes --exclude tests` clean. Checklist: B, G, I, J, K.

- **Two silent-success paths in the create path, plus stale contract prose (previous change)** — twelve review findings triaged against landed code; nine fixed, three deliberately not actioned.

  **The two real defects were both "reported success for something that did not happen".** `ApplyStyleOrDiscard` returned normally after discarding the shape, so `Create` carried on to stamp `Name` and `AlternativeText` onto a shape that was no longer on the sheet — a write that either escaped as a misleading second refusal or returned `Ok` for a shape that did not exist. It now returns the outcome, `Create` returns `HostRejected`, and the stamp is never attempted. The same class existed one level down: a text box is the one kind whose content is written *inside* `AddShape`, so a throwing `ApplyText` escaped the create as an unhandled COM error **and** left an untagged text box nothing could ever update or remove. `ApplyTextOrDiscard` now contains it and reports the same typed refusal.

  **The row selector's best-effort guard covered only `Activate`/`Select`, not the walk that reaches them.** A refused sheet, table-name, or list-row lookup escaped `SelectBodyRow` — and since the row is already inserted by then, that surfaces a *successful* insert to the user as a failure. The walk is now inside the guard. Two new positive tests inject a refusal at the list-row lookup and the table-name read; both were mutation-checked (narrowing the guard back to the two selection calls fails exactly the two degradation tests, and the pre-existing ones).

  **Not actioned, with reasons.** Batched name lookup for `FindShapeByName` is a performance change, not a defect — the current scan is the documented seam design and the reconciler's `IShapeWritePort` contract is what would have to change; it belongs in its own row. Releasing the `ListRows` proxy was declined: the class contract states every proxy reached from the captured workbook is an Excel-owned shared root that is never force-released, so releasing it would break the documented ownership policy. Holding it in a local (the reviewer's other half) was done.

  **Docs fixed for staleness, not style.** `REMEDIATION-PLAN-A.md` had Phases 7–9 and the tail of Phase 6 stranded *after* the Deferred table and the P1/P2 rows orphaned after the checklist — both restored to their sections. `REMEDIATION-PLAN-B.md` Problem item 4 ended mid-sentence with its continuation stranded at the document's end. `R4.7` Step 0 still asserted the create-only style premise that expired with R4.6 and contradicted its own D1; now marked superseded. `R4.11`'s "Visual / golden" section claimed two assertions were left failing on purpose — false since `e80bc8f` regenerated the golden, and it now requires completion and human review before merge. `SceneShapeRenderer.BuildText` and `ShapeReconcileAction` carried remarks that the landed code had already overtaken.

  **The R4.2 live selection test asserted nothing.** It called `CaptureSelection`, never moved the cursor, and only *printed* the restore records — so D3's best-effort restore passed whether or not the scope did anything. It now records the original address, selects `C7` inside the scope, asserts the address is restored afterwards, and requires an empty record list.

Every later step was run directly instead (build, full non-Office test sweep). `verify-office.ps1` **Not run**: the integration-test change is a live-host assertion and this environment has no live Excel gate, so the new selection-restore assertion is unexecuted here. Checklist: B, C, G, I, J, K — E and F are not claimed (no refresh or golden behaviour changed).

- **Refresh reconciliation is idempotent, and the contract's own "zero operations" pin turned out to be unachievable on this port (this change)** — R4.7 lands `ShapeReconciler` (a pure planner plus an executor) and the live double-refresh gate.

  **The work item's D1 had two stale premises, and finding them was most of the work.** D1 said a matched shape "must not update its fill, stroke, or pattern in place: those members are still create-only because R4.6 owns them" — but R4.6 landed in `6520f62` and `ExcelShapeWriter.Update` now calls `ApplyText` and `ApplyStyle`. The sentence was written as conditional ("would be wrong *until* R4.6 closes the style side"), so it self-discharged; update-in-place is now both correct and the architecture's stated preference.

  **The second premise was the real fork, and it is a soundness question, not a cost question.** D1 and the acceptance tests both required *unchanged-scene → zero operations*, which needs a property diff. `ListOwned()` returns identifiers only, so the planner cannot know whether a matched shape already matches. More importantly a read-back diff would be **unsound on this write model**: R4.11's `TextColour = null` means "leave the host font alone", so after a write the intent is no longer observable and a diff could not distinguish "we wrote black" from "the theme supplied black" from "we never wrote". A tolerance loose enough to cope would also hide real changes; too strict and the refresh never converges, which is worse than always-update because it looks like a diff engine while being one. Both amendments were escalated to the product owner and approved: idempotence is now proved as a **fixed point** (a converged chart re-plans identically and leaves the same owned set), which is a pure function of two collections, needs no tolerance and no host read-back, and is strictly stronger evidence than "this refresh happened to write nothing".

  **D3's pin is asserted on the read, not just the writes.** A blocked refresh issues no shape write *and never calls `ListOwned`* — a version that read first and decided afterwards would mutate nothing yet still behave differently, and "did nothing at all" is what the architecture states.

  **Both new pins were mutation-checked, and each mutation failed exactly one test:** removing the delete sort failed only the order-independence test; disabling the D3 guard failed only the blocked-refresh test. Three analyzer/quality errors raised during implementation were fixed rather than suppressed (IDE1006, IDE0072, IDE0046, CA2208), and the CA2208 fix revealed that passing a loop variable as a `paramName` was wrong, so the unreachable-action arm is now an explicit `InvalidOperationException` naming itself a planner defect.

  **L18 is decided: refuse and surface.** `Create` already refuses an identifier past `MaxShapeNameLength = 255` before any host call, so an over-long shared-owner identifier fails as a typed refusal reported with its identifier — not silently truncated, and no new contributor-set limit invented.

  Observed: Release `-warnaserror` **0 warnings / 0 errors**; Office contract **498/498** (486 before, +12); Architecture **84/84**; `verify-office: PASS` **46/46** (45 before) against Office `16.0.20326.20158` x64, ~7m29s; Core unchanged at **1011/1013** — the same two golden assertions R4.11 left red, still awaiting their separate human-reviewed regeneration commit.

  **Left open on purpose:** the Ribbon wiring and the application-state scope around `Reconcile` are **R4.9's**; the benchmark evidence D2 requires before any atomic full-replace fallback is **R4.10's**. The benchmark is also what would justify a future diff, and this row's reasoning says a *full* diff cannot be sound here regardless of its cost.

- **Label text colour now propagates end to end, activating §17's inside/outside switch (R4.11, `e286dfe`)** — R4.6 closed with a recorded gap: "`SceneStyle` has no text-colour member, so a label inside a delay event cannot express the `DelayText` token… this is owed to a scene-model row." That row is **R4.11**, and this is it.

  **The break spanned four layers, and the two obvious ones were not the whole of it.** `GanttResolvedStyle` discarded the colour the named style had declared, `SceneStyle` had no member to hold it, `OfficeShapeRequest` had no member to carry it, and `ApplyText` never wrote it. But the *fifth* finding is the one that made §17 unreachable rather than merely unimplemented: **`LabelPlanner` already implemented the inside/outside switch correctly and completely — and both `LabelRequest` call sites omitted `OutsideTextStyle`**, so `request.OutsideTextStyle ?? request.TextStyle` always took the right-hand branch. The planner was never the broken part. No planner logic changed; `SceneBuilder` now supplies the argument it was written to receive.

  **Two open questions from the prior investigation are closed, and one is a non-event worth recording.** `DefaultText` needed no new code-owned definition: it already exists in `GanttCatalogues.Colours` (`#000000`) and in the guide's token table, so nothing was added, extended, or duplicated. And the interop member was verified by reflection against the installed `office.dll` 16.0.0 rather than from memory: `TextRange2.Font` → `Font2.Fill` → `FillFormat.ForeColor` → `ColorFormat.RGB`. `Font2` has **no** `Color` member — the fill is the only colour surface on it. The font fill also needs `Solid()` before a `ForeColor` write, or the host silently ignores the colour; that is now in the code and in the live test.

  **The live gate caught an error, and the error was in the test.** The new live test failed on its first run, reading back `0x123456` where it expected `0x563412`. Rather than adjust the expectation, I probed five tokens against the host: the font fill reports the **identical** packed value as the shape fill for every one, so the mapper was right and the test helper was double-reversing the bytes. This is R4.6's D4 pattern repeating, and it is the reason the work item's Step-0 table records the probe result rather than a claim. Nothing in the production path was changed to make the test pass.

  **A design correction, stated because it affects the type's future shape:** `SceneStyle`'s members are all get-only and validated in its constructor, so `with` cannot set a new one. Adding an `init` accessor would have been the smaller diff, but it would have let a copy bypass the validation that rejects a negative outline width or a non-positive font size. `SceneStyle.WithTextColour` rebuilds through the constructor instead.

  **`null` means unresolved throughout, never black.** A style that declares no text colour yields a null that is preserved to the host as "leave the font alone". Substituting `DefaultText` in the adapter would silently restyle every label whose style simply did not name a colour, in the renderer, which is the substitution the scene-first rule forbids.

  **The golden scene snapshot is left failing on purpose, and the delta is measured.** Adding a style member changes the serialised scene, so `The_built_fixture_scene_matches_the_committed_golden_snapshot` and its round-trip pair now fail. The D4 policy requires regeneration to be a separate human-reviewed commit, so the byte-for-byte assertion was **not** loosened. **Measured delta: 58 style objects each gain one `TextColour` field — 32 null, 26 `#000000`; no geometry moves and no primitive is added or removed.** Regeneration is the next commit and needs a human to review that number before it lands.

  **Also found and recorded, not fixed here:** a malformed text colour is rejected by `GanttStyleDefinition`'s constructor, not by the resolver — the resolver's own colour parse is defence-in-depth behind that check, exactly as the fill and stroke guards already were. My first test asserted the refusal at the resolver and failed for this reason; the test now asserts the real boundary. This is recorded because it is the kind of fact that otherwise gets "fixed" by deleting a guard.

The 2 Core failures are the golden assertions above and are expected. `verify-quick.ps1`: every step PASS **except** `test (OfficeIntegration excluded)`, which fails on those same 2 golden assertions. Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I. **F not certified** — golden regeneration is deliberately outstanding.

- **The live Office gate runs green on the splitter-border change, and it establishes what the fix does and does not yet do (this change)** — I previously wrote that this gate "needs desktop Excel and can't be simulated here". **That was wrong and I checked it rather than leaving it standing:** this machine has Office `16.0.20326.20158` x64 (`O365HomePremRetail`, `en-us`) installed, so the gate was runnable. `pwsh ./scripts/verify-office.ps1` → **`verify-office: PASS`, 43/43, 0 failed**.

  The COM-proxy leak signal came in at **26 forced kills against a ceiling of 26**. That is the documented non-determinism, not a regression: the script's own comment records identical trees producing 24, 25 and 26, and the only Office-layer file this change touched is `IShapeWritePort.cs`, where the diff is **XML documentation only** — it cannot affect process teardown. So the signal is at the ratchet boundary, not above it, and nothing here is evidence for or against raising it further.

  **The more useful result is what the live host revealed about the fix itself.** I expected the §10 border to become visible after this change, and the live run shows it does not — and the reason is worth recording rather than glossing. `SceneShapeRenderer.BuildLine` translates a `SceneLine` into an `OfficeShapeRequest` carrying **only** its id, kind, endpoints and z-layer; it passes no `StrokeColour` and no `LineWidthPt`. The Office layer contains **no** `LineWeight`, `ForeColor`, or `Fill.ForeColor` member anywhere. So the corrected border style is correct **in the scene** and is still not written to the host, and the border renders at whatever default weight Excel chooses.

  **This is R4.6's unbuilt scope, not a defect in this change.** R4.6 is titled "Map all scene styles including planned, actual, baseline, critical, and hatch patterns" and its Scope names "the complete token→property mapping (fill colour/alpha, line colour/width, pattern, font family/size/bold) over the R4.3–R4.5 paths" as exactly this work. The distinction matters for review: this change fixed the **scene**, which was genuinely wrong — it had silently discarded a validated `BorderWidthPt` — while the **live** stroke remains correctly out of scope and pinned to R4.6. Had the scene been left as it was, R4.6 would have had nothing correct to map, because the border style it would have read carried a fill and a null stroke. **The live gate therefore does not certify the border's appearance, and this STATUS entry should not be read as doing so.**

This is that commit. The human review the policy asks for was given on the measured delta before this file was touched.

  **Verified delta: 2 primitives changed, style-only, 58 primitives before and after.** `G-000000000000000000000000000000a1:splitter-band:top` and `:bottom` move from `StyleKey: Splitter`, `FillColour: #FFE699`, `StrokeColour: null`, `OutlineWidthPt: null` to `StyleKey: MajorGrid`, `FillColour: null`, `StrokeColour: #808080`, `OutlineWidthPt: 1`. These are the only two changed lines in the file.

  **The delta was checked field by field, not eyeballed**, against a copy of the previous baseline: the primitive-ID sets are identical, a normalised comparison of every primitive with its `Style` masked out reported **zero** differences, the `Warnings` array is byte-identical, and `ChartBounds` is unchanged. So no geometry, ordering, text, or z-layer value moved — which is what a style-only fix must produce, and what would *not* have been acceptable to wave through.

Here the output copy was **deleted** and the suite re-run so MSBuild re-copied the committed file: Core **999/999**. A passing golden test is only evidence when the file under test is known to be the committed one.

  The regeneration harness was **temporary and has been removed** before this commit. The byte-for-byte assertion is unchanged.

- **Review findings pass: one real scene defect fixed, seven documentation corrections, and the golden is left for human review (this change)** — Eight review comments were checked against the current tree; all eight were still valid. Seven were documentation that the tree had already moved past, and **one was a live defect** rather than a stale claim.

  **The defect: `SplitterBuilder` discarded the §10 border width it had already validated.** Both border `SceneLine`s reused `request.Style`, which is the **band fill** preset, so each border carried `StrokeColour: null` and `OutlineWidthPt: null`. §10 specifies "Its top and bottom borders are `MajorBoundaryPt` lines", so the scene described a major boundary no renderer could draw, and the `BorderWidthPt` the caller supplied — and that `TryBuild` validated a few lines earlier — was silently dropped. The committed golden had been carrying the same wrong value (`StyleKey: Splitter`, both stroke members null), which is why reading it as authoritative would have hidden this rather than exposed it.

  The band and its borders now take their styles from different sources, because §10 specifies them differently: the band is a **fill** and keeps the resolved `SplitterFill` preset, each border is a **stroke** taking its colour from a newly supplied `BorderStyle` and its width from the structural `BorderWidthPt`. That is exactly the split ADR-0022 D4 draws, so the fix makes the code agree with a decision that was already recorded. `SceneBuilder` passes the frame theme's `MajorGrid` style, so a splitter border is stroked by the same authority as the chart frame lines rather than by a second literal colour. A null `BorderStyle` is refused as `NullDependency` rather than defaulting a stroke.

  **Non-vacuity is proven, not asserted:** reverting the width source to the style's own value fails the new test with `Expected: 1.5 / Actual: 99`, because the test's `_borderStyle` deliberately declares a width of 99 to prove the structural token wins. A second mutation that re-pointed both border lines at `request.Style` was caught by IDE0059 before it reached a test, which is the analyzer doing the same job. The temporary scene-dump harness used to measure the delta was removed before this commit.

  **The golden is deliberately NOT regenerated.** The measured delta is **2 primitives, style-only, zero geometry/order/warning changes**: `…a1:splitter-band:top` and `…a1:splitter-band:bottom` move from `Splitter`/fill-only to `MajorGrid`/`#808080`/width 1. Primitive count stays 58. Per the D4 golden policy, regeneration is a separate human-reviewed commit, so `The_built_fixture_scene_matches_the_committed_golden_snapshot` is the **one expected failure** and the gate is doing its job.

  Documentation corrections: the R3 remediation note in the roadmap claimed the golden was "deliberately not yet regenerated" when it was regenerated twice in reviewed commits `4278a49` and `4c29675`, and that all three remaining items were open when commits B, C, and D closed them; REPO-MAP's final panel-measurement sentence still said mixed heights were refused and per-row measurement was future work, which ADR-0024 falsified, and the commit-C and commit-D records were added. R4.4's Step 0 still carried the falsified `xlAlignLeft`/`xlAlignCenter`/`xlAlignRight` spellings and omitted the `TextFrame2` path, contradicting its own D3. R4.5's D1, Outcome, Step 0, stop condition, risk paragraph, and evidence-ledger row all still presented the freeform path as current, which the measured supersession replaced with `OfficeShapeKind.Diamond`. R4.7's D1 claimed an in-place "geometry/style/text" update, contradicting the create-only fill/stroke/pattern limitation its own Step 0 records. R4.8's ledger row said only "`GanttCreator.` tag" and cited no ADR, where the filter is the `GanttCreator.Owned.v1:` prefix in alternative text (ADR-0019). `IShapeWritePort.ApplyZOrder` still documented the retired forward-moving algorithm instead of the preflight-then-commit reverse `msoSendToBack` pass that landed in `e5211ac`.
