Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I.

- **R4.6 — every style token now reaches the host, and it turns out almost none of them did (this change)** — Closes the row the remediation brief lists as its item 10 ("R4.6 makes style updates complete before R4.7 relies on Update"). Three separate gaps, all confirmed by reading the landed code rather than inferred.

  **Gap 1 — the renderer dropped the style for every family except text.** `SceneShapeRenderer.BuildRect`, `BuildLine`, and `BuildDiamond` built requests carrying geometry and `ZLayer` only; `BuildText` alone forwarded `SceneStyle`. A planned bar, an as-built bar, a critical overlay, a delineator, and a milestone diamond all reached `ExcelShapeWriter` with **no fill, no stroke, and no width at all**. The four builds now map the scene's resolved style verbatim.

  **Gap 2 — `OfficeShapeRequest` had no `HatchPattern` member,** so the procurement hatch token could not cross the port regardless of what the renderer did. Added, typed as Core's own `GanttHatchPattern` so the token table and the host mapping cannot drift.

  **Gap 3 — `ExcelShapeWriter` never wrote fill, stroke colour, or line width.** `Create` and `Update` wrote geometry and (for text boxes) typography. Every shape landed in whatever accent colour Excel defaults an auto-shape to. This is the finding that matters: the Phase-4 render path had been producing correct *positions* for entirely unstyled shapes, and every geometry test in the suite passed regardless.

  **The mapping is split by concern, deliberately.** `OfficeStyleMapper` is a pure, interop-free function from the request to `OfficeShapeStyle`, so the whole matrix is assertable without a host and the AddIn compilation still never names the PIA for a style value. `ExcelShapeWriter.ApplyStyle` owns the COM and is the only interop surface. `ApplyStyle` runs on **both** `Create` and `Update` — the update half is what closes the staleness class R4.4 opened for text, since a refresh that changes only a colour would otherwise move the shape and leave the old colour behind. On `Create` the style is applied *before* the ownership stamp so a shape that cannot be styled is discarded rather than left half-written.

  **Step-0 probe (the row's obligation), all against the installed interop assembly.** `MsoPatternType` provides `msoPatternLightDownwardDiagonal`, `msoPatternLightUpwardDiagonal`, and `msoPatternDiagonalCross` — forward, backward, and cross are each expressible and distinct. A colour is written through `ColorFormat.RGB` as `blue * 65536 + green * 256 + red` (MS-OI29500 6.1.2.7.1.11), the inverse of the `#RRGGBB` form the tokens are authored in. Alpha is **not** on the colour: it becomes `Transparency`, whose 0-opaque scale is the inverse of the token's alpha. Theme colours are never touched. **`IShape.Fill` is `Excel.FillFormat` and there is no `FillFormat2` in this PIA** — an assumption corrected before it reached code.

  **One recorded gap, not an approximation: `HatchPitchPt` and `HatchLinePt` are not honoured.** `MsoPatternType` is a fixed set of raster patterns with no member controlling pitch or stroke width. That is precisely the "compatibility test" entity-guide §18 anticipates, run: it does not prove equivalence, so the deviation is recorded for R8.3 rather than faked, and §18's editable-hatch-lines fallback stays available. An unmapped `GanttHatchPattern` member **throws** rather than drawing the nearest pattern.

  **Two tests caught my own errors, which is the point of writing the live one.** A contract test caught the mapper setting `FillVisible = false` for a line while still populating `FillRgb` — a record advertising a colour for a shape with no fill, a trap for the next caller; the colour is now nulled. The live test then caught a **wrong assertion of mine**: I asserted `msoFillPatterned` for every filled shape, and a solid fill correctly reports `msoFillSolid`. The colour itself came back exactly right (`0x50D092`), so the code was correct and the test was not. The fill type is now asserted per case, and the hatch row asserts *patterned* specifically so it cannot pass on a host that silently dropped the hatch.

  The matrix rows are transcribed from the guide's tables as **literals**, and two completeness guards assert the covered set equals `GanttCatalogues.Colours` and `GanttCatalogues.StylePresets` — so "the full matrix" fails when a twenty-second token or a sixteenth preset lands without a row, rather than quietly becoming the matrix as it was when written.

  `pwsh ./scripts/verify-office.ps1` → **`verify-office: PASS`**, 44/44 (43 → 44), Office `16.0.20326.20158` x64 `O365HomePremRetail` `en-us`. Every row — planned, actual, baseline, critical, procurement-hatched, splitter, spacer, critical milestone, delineator — read back off the live shape as the exact token value. Non-Office: Core 999/999, Office contract **476/476** (374 → 476), Architecture 84/84, AddIn 201/201, Raster 41/41. Release `/warnaserror` 0 warnings. COM-proxy signal **26 against a ceiling of 26** — at, not over; the ratchet docs record the signal as load-dependent, and the new test sets `SuppressLeakSignal` for the same reason the other render tests do. Checklist: A, C, E, I, J.

- **The live Office gate runs green, and it found a defect no contract test could (previous change)** — `pwsh ./scripts/verify-office.ps1` → **`verify-office: PASS`**, 43/43, against Office `16.0.20326.20158` (x64, `O365HomePremRetail`, `en-us`). The COM-proxy leak signal is **25 against a ceiling of 26**, i.e. below the previous baseline rather than raised. The first attempt at this gate **failed 2/44**, and what it found is the entry's substance.

  **The defect: a freshly initialised `tblGanttData` has a header row and no data rows, so `DataBodyRange` is null and the measurement is correctly refused — but both live tests were measuring exactly that empty table and treating the resulting `InvalidMeasurement` as the property under test.** The pre-existing mixed-height live test was written before commit `2b84689`, and an earlier STATUS entry recorded it as "written and compiles but unexecuted", so the mistake had never met the host. The mocked contract tests could not have caught it: they supply their own row list, so the precondition that matters most was never modelled.

  Diagnosis was by a **temporary live probe** that read each dependency directly through the interop API — `ShowHeaders=True`, `HeaderRowRange.RowHeight = 15`, `ListRows.Count = 0`, `DataBodyRange` null, `body.Rows.Count`, each `rows.Item[i].RowHeight`, `column.Range.Width = 48` — rather than by inference. The probe was **deleted**; it is not part of the suite.

  **Host evidence now recorded, all of it previously assumed or unknown:**
  - `DataBodyRange.RowHeight` on a genuinely mixed body returns **`DBNull`** on this build. The `DBNull` concern behind the old conversion guard was real, and the per-row walk handles it without consulting the aggregate.
  - A four-row body set to 15/45/24 measured back as **`15/25/35/45`** — the per-row read is exact, and the values are asserted, not just the success flag.
  - **Measurement succeeds on a protected worksheet.** This was the single unknown commit `2b84689` was shipped without, and it is now host-confirmed rather than documented assumption.
  - An empty table refuses as `InvalidMeasurement`, now asserted in the same session as its own precondition rather than as an accident.

  **The COM-proxy ratchet was respected, not worked around.** The protected-sheet check is a genuine second claim, but giving it its own Excel instance pushed the signal to 30 and then 27 against a ceiling of 26. Rather than raise the ceiling — which the ratchet documentation explicitly forbids without evidence — the three claims were **merged into one live session**, which is why the suite is 43 tests rather than 45 and the signal is 25. The protected block unprotects in a `finally` so a locked sheet is not left for teardown.

  Non-Office: Core 997/997, Office contract 374/374, Architecture 84/84. `verify-quick.ps1` and `verify.ps1` **Not run** (tree not clean when this entry was written). Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I, J, K.

- **The golden scene snapshot is regenerated after approval, and a degenerate primitive it exposed is fixed (this change)** — Closes the gate left open by `11df4e1`. The delta was presented and approved: the reference scene is deliberately **panel-free** and previously supplied an arbitrary `PanelBounds` purely to give the chart a wide content union, so with that input removed the chart is correctly the plot plus its header bands. `chart:background` moves from `0,6 1120x394` to `520,60 600x340`, the four `chart:frame:*` lines and the title band follow, and the §10 splitter band narrows from 1120pt to 600pt. No entity geometry moved.

  **Inspecting the regenerated baseline found a real defect, which is why this is not a one-line regeneration.** The new scene contained a `splitter-label` with `TextBounds.Width: 0`. With no data panel, §10's `DataPanelLeft` position has nowhere to sit — the band starts at the plot's own left edge, so the available width is exactly zero — and the builder emitted an invisible text primitive that a renderer must still place and reconciliation must track forever. A blank or degenerate primitive is the same class of defect this repository treats as a bug, so `SplitterBuilder` now **suppresses** a label with no room rather than emitting it at zero width, and a test pins it. The golden therefore loses that primitive: **59 → 58 primitives, one removed** (`…a1:splitter-label`), which is the only removal across the whole remediation.

  **A false green was caught and is worth recording.** After the fix, the Core suite reported 998/998 — but that run compared against a **stale build-output copy** of the golden, not the committed file, because the test reads the copy MSBuild placed in the output directory. Deleting that copy and re-running made the same test fail, proving the committed golden was stale. The baseline was regenerated from the fixed code, and the suite re-verified with a freshly-copied golden: **997/997**. The temporary regeneration harness was removed before this commit and the byte-for-byte assertion was never loosened. **The lesson generalises: a passing golden test is only evidence when the file under test is known to be the committed one.**

  Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I.

- **Remediation commit C — the live adapter measures each row, and a read-only measurement no longer refuses a protected sheet (this change)** — Closes the gap commit `11df4e1` deliberately left open, planned in [`docs/REMEDIATION-PLAN-C.md`](REMEDIATION-PLAN-C.md) and recorded in [`docs/adr/0024-per-row-measurement-and-read-only-protection.md`](adr/0024-per-row-measurement-and-read-only-protection.md). Commit B made the Core contract able to describe a body whose rows differ in height; the adapter could not yet supply one, so the capability was unusable end to end.

  **The body aggregate is no longer consulted at all.** `ReadRowHeight` read `DataBodyRange.RowHeight` and then demanded every row equal it. The `Range.RowHeight` reference states a range of differing heights "might return the height of the first row or might return Null" — `Null`/`DBNull` was catchable, which is why the earlier fix existed, but **the "first row's height" case is a plain number that passed every check**. A 15pt/45pt body reporting 15pt previously produced a grid laid out entirely at 15pt. The adapter now walks the body rows and reads each row's own height, and `ConfirmUniformRowHeight`/`HasUniformRowHeight` are **deleted rather than relaxed**: the aggregate is unreliable in *both* documented directions, so detecting the bad case is strictly worse than never reading the value. The header row is measured separately from `HeaderRowRange`, which is what §4 requires and what commit B's new field was for. One unreadable row refuses the whole measurement, because the panel's row positions are cumulative and a single defaulted height would displace every row below it while looking plausible.

  **A read-only adapter no longer refuses a protected worksheet** (the brief's `P2-1`). It mutates nothing, and Excel's protection blocks writes rather than reads — column widths and row heights are readable on a protected sheet — so the consultation made a read-only measurement, the input to a read-only diagnostic or export, unavailable on any protected target. That is a capability restriction with no product behind it. **Enforcement moves to the Refresh/write boundary, which is R4.9's layer**; that row is not built, so nothing regresses and no protected target can be written, because nothing renders yet. `NoActiveWorkbook` is unaffected — the adapter reads `ActiveWorkbook` directly rather than inferring it from a guard query — and a new positive test pins that. The guard **parameter was removed rather than kept and ignored**: an accepted-but-unused parameter is either dead state or a held field that reads as "this adapter checks protection", which is exactly the misconception being removed.

  **The brief's claim that this required a `ProtectionGuardFirstTests` update is wrong, and was checked rather than assumed.** That test classifies Office sources by *data mutation*, and this file is already listed as read-only; removing a guard call cannot change that. The architecture suite passes 84/84, unchanged.

  Office contract **374/374** (372 → 374; two mixed-body tests added, the protected-target refusal test **deleted and replaced** by a positive test, and the `TargetProtected` path removed). Architecture 84/84 confirms the classification claim. The live integration test previously asserted a mixed body was *refused*; it now asserts success and the exact per-row values, and a second live test covers a protected worksheet. **`verify-office.ps1` Not run** — both live tests are unexecuted, so the per-row behaviour and the protected-read claim rest on contract tests and code reading, not on the host. The protected-read premise is the one thing that genuinely needs a live run before R4.6, and the new test is written to fail loudly if a future Office build disagrees. `verify-quick.ps1` and `verify.ps1` **Not run** (tree not clean when this entry was written). Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I, K.

- **Remediation commit B — the data panel measures per row, its bounds are derived, and its rows come from the source table (this change)** — The Core panel contract, planned in [`docs/REMEDIATION-PLAN-B.md`](REMEDIATION-PLAN-B.md) and recorded in [`docs/adr/0023-panel-measures-per-row.md`](adr/0023-panel-measures-per-row.md). **Four defects, all confirmed against source before the plan was written, and a fifth found while implementing the first three.**

  `PanelCellGrid` carried a single `RowHeightPt` for the whole body, and `PanelBuilder` positioned cells at `bodyTop + (rowIndex * height)`. §3 requires the exact measured cell bounds, so a body with differing row heights could not be reproduced — and the live adapter *refused* such a table rather than misplacing it. The grid now carries `RowHeightsPt` (one per body row, positional) and its own `HeaderHeightPt`, which §4 requires as a separate measurement because the header is its own worksheet row. `RowHeightPt` is **removed**, not deprecated.

  **The panel's bounds had two authorities.** `SceneBuildRequest.PanelBounds` fed `FrameBandsBuilder` while `PanelBuilder` independently derived the panel, and nothing checked they agreed. The input is gone: the panel is built **first** and the frame consumes `PanelBuildResult.PanelBounds`. This is possible because `PanelBuilder` needs only the grid, the rows, the plot bounds, and the period-header bottom — all known before the frame exists. `BuildSplitters` therefore moved after the panel, since §10's band starts at the panel's left edge.

  **The fifth defect, found during implementation:** panel rows were derived from `placements.Placements`, so any row that is not an ordinary lane-placed event — `Splitter`, `Spacer`, `Delineator`, or a hidden row — **could not appear in the data panel at all**. Commit `4537b86` made the structural rows lane participants, so this became visibly wrong rather than latent. A new pure `PanelRowProjection` now owns the mapping: every validated source row gets a panel row, in `RowNumber` order. **Worksheet order, not the plot's `SortOrder`-then-row lane order** — the two differ once a user sets `SortOrder`, and sorting the panel by lane order would make the data panel disagree with the table beside it. **A hidden row now renders a panel row where it previously did not**; this is a product-observable change, and it is the guide-faithful reading ("visible approved columns" qualifies columns, and `Visible` "suppresses entity and its label"), stated here rather than left to be discovered in a diff.

  A new `PanelBuildRefusal.RowCountMismatch` guards the positional alignment: a source row that failed validation can no longer shift every panel cell below it. Two validators were **removed** as unreachable rather than left as decoration — `PanelBuildRefusal.NonPositiveHeaderHeight` (the grid validates the header height, so it could never fire; its test moved to `PanelCellGridTests`) and the old zero-row panel path, which is now unrepresentable because a header with no body reads as a data table that lost its data.

  **The Office adapter still refuses a mixed body.** The Core contract can now express one; the adapter cannot yet measure one, so it replicates its one confirmed-uniform height to keep compiling. **That gap is the next change** and naming it here is the point — a correct contract is what makes the gap visible instead of latent. It is not a silent partial fix.

  **The golden scene's chart bounds change, and the delta is presented for approval:** 59 primitives before and after, none added or removed, **no entity geometry moved**. Only eleven primitives differ, all consequences of one fact: the reference scene is deliberately **panel-free**, and it previously supplied an arbitrary `PanelBounds` of `(0, 20, 520, 200)` purely to give the chart a wide content union. With that input gone, a panel-free chart is the plot plus its header bands, so `chart:background` shrinks from `0,6 1120x394` to `520,60 600x340`, the four `chart:frame:*` lines and the title band follow, and the §10 splitter band narrows from 1120pt to 600pt wide (with no panel, plot-only is §10's correct degenerate). **Whether the canonical reference scene should instead be given a real panel now that the panel is properly derived is a separate question this change does not decide.**

  Core **995/996**, Architecture 84/84, Office contract 372/372. The one failure is the golden, and it is the intended gate. New tests: `tests/GanttCreator.Core.Tests/Scene/PanelRowProjectionTests.cs`, mixed-height layout and `RowCountMismatch` pins in `PanelBuilderTests`, and per-row/header-height pins in `PanelCellGridTests`. `verify-quick.ps1` and `verify.ps1` **Not run** (tree not clean when this entry was written). **`verify-office.ps1` Not run** — the adapter's behaviour is unchanged in this commit. Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I.

- **Remediation commit D — `ApplyZOrder` will no longer reorder a shape it does not own (this change)** — Closes the R4.7 ownership blocker recorded in [`docs/REMEDIATION-PLAN-GOLDEN-AND-D.md`](REMEDIATION-PLAN-GOLDEN-AND-D.md). `Update`, `Delete`, and `ListOwned` all authorise on `CarriesOwnershipTagFor`, but `ApplyZOrder` resolved shapes **by name** and called `ZOrder` with no ownership check at all. A user-drawn shape that happened to carry a requested primitive ID would therefore have been *reordered* by a refresh, while the identical shape is correctly preserved by every other mutation path — a guarantee that held everywhere else and silently did not hold on this one. The entity guide requires refresh to touch only shapes whose alternative text carries a valid tag.

  **The change is smaller than the finding implies, and deliberately so:** the method already had a preflight that resolved every named shape before issuing the first command, because a missing shape must not leave a partial order. The ownership filter was simply missing from that same loop, so it was added there rather than as a new pass. An unowned shape anywhere in the list now refuses with **zero** `ZOrder` calls, under the same `NotFound` reason `Update` and `Delete` already report — an unowned shape is deliberately indistinguishable from an absent one, because re-stamping its tag would "repair" it, which is R9.4's job to report. **The approved single reverse-order `msoSendToBack` pass is untouched**: the change is *who* may be reordered, never *how*.

  **The mutation check found two vacuous tests, which is the reason this entry is worth reading.** The three refusal tests were first written against `writer.ZOrderCommands`, and moving the ownership test into the mutation loop left all three **passing**. The reason: that recorder is wired only into shapes `AddShape` creates, so a test built from seeded shapes observed zero commands regardless of behaviour. `NewShape` now takes an optional `onZOrder` callback, mirroring the existing `onDelete` convention, and the assertions read a log attached to the shapes themselves. A second defect surfaced in the test *design*: the atomicity test was originally written as "an unowned shape **last** in the list", which cannot work — the approved pass walks the list in **reverse**, so a per-shape check processes the last entry first, refuses, and issues no command whether or not the check is preflighted. Rewritten with the unowned shape in the **middle**, where a per-shape check has already moved a shape by the time it refuses.

  Non-vacuity is now proven rather than asserted: with the ownership test moved into the mutation loop, **5 tests fail** (`The_z_order_pass_refuses_an_unowned_same_name_shape_and_moves_nothing`, `An_unowned_shape_in_the_middle_leaves_the_surrounding_owned_shapes_untouched`, `A_shape_carrying_another_primitives_valid_tag_is_still_refused_by_the_z_order_pass`, `The_z_order_pass_still_applies_the_approved_sequence_when_every_shape_is_owned`, and the pre-existing `The_z_order_pass_sends_each_shape_to_the_back_in_reverse_...`). With the check restored, all pass. The pre-existing reverse-order pin and its `DoesNotContain("BringToFront")` assertion are unchanged, so the approved algorithm is demonstrably not what moved.

  Office contract **372/372** (367 → 372, five new tests). The R4.7 work item records the preflight as discharged. `verify-quick.ps1` and `verify.ps1` **Not run** (tree not clean when this entry was written). **`verify-office.ps1` Not run** — no host behaviour is observably changed without a real shape, and this row's live gate is a double-refresh exercise that belongs to R4.7 proper; a run is nonetheless recommended before R4.6 because the scene R4.3–R4.5 consume changed in commit `4537b86`. Checklist: E, I, K.

- **The golden scene snapshot is regenerated after human approval (this change)** — Commit `4537b86` made the fixture's `Splitter` and `Spacer` reach lane layout, so the committed baseline had to change: those rows already existed in `tests/fixtures/reference-gantt.json` and the old `SceneBuilder` discarded them, meaning the baseline had never contained §10 geometry. The delta was presented for review and **approved** before this file was touched, per the D4 golden policy that regeneration is a separate, human-reviewed commit. The plan and the approved delta are recorded in [`docs/REMEDIATION-PLAN-GOLDEN-AND-D.md`](REMEDIATION-PLAN-GOLDEN-AND-D.md).

  **Verified delta (55 → 59 primitives; chart bounds unchanged at 1120x394):** four primitives added, none removed — `…a1:splitter-band` (layer 30, y=110, h=18, w=1120), `…a1:splitter-band:top` and `…a1:splitter-band:bottom` (layer 80), `…a1:splitter-label` (layer 70). Eleven geometry primitives moved by **exactly +18pt** (= `SplitterHeightPt`): bars `b1` 113→131, `b2` 123→141, `b3` 133→151, `d1` 167→185, `e1` 177→195, `e2` 187→205, critical overlay `c1` 113→131, milestone markers `f1`–`f4` 201/211/221/231 → 219/229/239/249. The arithmetic was checked rather than assumed: the `Splitter` is the fixture's first row so every later lane is displaced once, and the `Spacer` is the last row so its 9pt displaces nothing. A displacement that was not a whole `SplitterHeightPt` would have indicated a geometry defect.

  **A second change was found during verification and is also intended:** 21 primitives moved from `LaneOrder` *n* to *n*+1, because the `Splitter` now occupies lane 0 and pushes each following lane down one index. A normalised field-by-field comparison of the regenerated file against its predecessor reported **zero** differences outside Y, `LaneOrder`, and polygon `Points`, so nothing unintended — no colour, text, width, or ordering-value change — rode along with the regeneration.

  Core **987/987**, Architecture 84/84. The two determinism pins that would catch a baseline which is merely self-consistent rather than correct both pass: `Shuffled_fixture_rows_produce_an_identical_scene` and `The_golden_snapshot_round_trips_through_deserialization`. The regeneration harness was temporary and **was removed before this commit**; the byte-for-byte assertion was never loosened, which is the property the policy exists to protect. `verify-quick.ps1` and `verify.ps1` **Not run** (the tree was not clean when this entry was written). **`verify-office.ps1` Not run** — no production code changed. Checklist: G, I.

- **Remediation commit A — Splitter/Spacer lane geometry is reachable, the §10 band is implemented, and a delineator-only scene is no longer "empty" (this change)** — Closed the R3 composition defects recorded in [`docs/REMEDIATION-PLAN-A.md`](REMEDIATION-PLAN-A.md), which re-verified every claim in the external remediation brief against source before acting. **Three of the brief's findings were incomplete or wrong and are corrected here rather than implemented as written.** (1) The brief said `ApplyZOrder` should preflight ownership but did not note that `LaneLayoutBuilder` also refuses an empty input, so fixing only `SceneBuilder` left a delineator-only scene refused as `InvalidLayoutSettings`; both sites were changed. (2) The brief described the approved z-order pass as "SendToBack + BringForward"; the landed code is **reverse-order `msoSendToBack` only**, with no `BringForward`, and STATUS had been quoting a test name that commit `41fa0cc` renamed. The algorithm is untouched by this change. (3) The brief asserted the read-only protection fix would require updating `ProtectionGuardFirstTests`; that test classifies Office adapters by *data mutation*, not by guard calls, so no architecture-test change is needed — that item is deferred as an ADR-0008 policy decision rather than actioned.

  **`SceneBuilder` now partitions visible rows into `laneParticipants` (including `Splitter` and `Spacer`) and `plotGlobalEntities` (`Delineator`)** instead of one "renderable" list that excluded all three. `LaneLayoutBuilder.BuildFixedLane` — the §10/§11 fixed-height lane branch — was **unreachable from the top level**, so §10 geometry was dead code its own unit tests could not detect. Emptiness now means "no visible scene-producing entity", and `LaneLayoutRefusal.EmptyInput` is **removed** rather than deprecated: a geometry builder refusing "no lanes" is what forced the orchestrator to report a renderable chart as empty. Two tests that encoded the old behaviour are rewritten — the `EmptyInput` case and `A_splitter_and_spacer_alone_are_refused_because_neither_has_an_entity_primitive`; they were evidence of a defect, not of a contract.

  **A third defect the brief does not name, found and fixed here:** `LaneOrdering.LaneKey` returned `LaneId.Value` verbatim while `LaneLayoutBuilder` picks its lane branch from the first input in a group, so a `Splitter` sharing a `LaneId` with an activity was built as an ordinary event lane, contradicting §10's "a complete lane". `LaneKey` now returns a row-scoped key for a `Splitter`/`Spacer`.

  **New `src/GanttCreator.Core/Scene/SplitterBuilder.cs`** implements the §10 band from the data panel's left edge to the plot's right edge at `ZLayer.Section` (30), with `MajorBoundaryPt` top/bottom borders at `ZLayer.Frame` (80), and the four permitted label positions — `DataPanelLeft` (default), `PlotCentre`, `Both` (two role-derived texts, per §10), and `None`. No new token was introduced: `SplitterFill`, `SplitterHeightPt`, and `MajorBoundaryPt` already existed and nothing consumed them. **One real defect was introduced and caught during this work:** the lane layout is lane-relative (first lane at `y=0`), so passing `lane.Top` raw put the band in the header bands and its label outside the chart — surfaced by `SceneValidator`'s `LabelOutsideChart` finding, not by a review. The offset is now applied once at the call site, matching `BuildSpans`.
  **The delineator style now has one authority:** the fallback no longer restates `#404040` inline. `DefaultDelineatorStyle` became `CataloguePresetStyle`, which reads the `DefaultDelineator` preset, so the `DelineatorStroke` token is the single source of truth. `Splitter`/`Spacer`/`Delineator` all resolve from the code-owned preset, because `GanttStyleResolver` deliberately refuses a blank `StyleKey` and these three types carry no named style in the workbook registry — requiring registry resolution refused valid workbooks (caught by the reference fixture, which contains all three).

  Observed: Core 986/987 pass, Architecture 84/84, Release solution build 0 warnings/0 errors. **The one Core failure is the golden scene snapshot, and it is the intended Phase-7 gate, not a defect:** `The_built_fixture_scene_matches_the_committed_golden_snapshot` now reports `…a1:splitter-band…` where the golden had `…b3:bar…`. The full primitive-level delta is presented for human review below and **the golden has deliberately not been regenerated** — per the D4 policy that is a separate, human-approved commit. `verify-quick.ps1` and `verify.ps1` **Not run** (this entry is written before the commit, so the tree is not clean and `verify.ps1` requires one). **`verify-office.ps1` Not run** — no Office code changed, but this change alters the scene R4.3–R4.5 consume and a run is recommended before R4.6. Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I.

  **Golden delta awaiting approval (55 → 59 primitives; chart bounds unchanged at 1120x394):** four primitives **added**, none removed — `…a1:splitter-band` (layer 30, y=110, h=18, w=1120), `…a1:splitter-band:top` and `…a1:splitter-band:bottom` (layer 80), and `…a1:splitter-label` (layer 70). Every geometry primitive **below** the Splitter moved by **exactly +18pt** (= `SplitterHeightPt`): bars `b1` 113→131, `b2` 123→141, `b3` 133→151, `d1` 167→185, `e1` 177→195, `e2` 187→205, critical overlay `c1` 113→131, and milestone markers `f1`–`f4` 201/211/221/231 → 219/229/239/249. The arithmetic is explained by the fixture: the `Splitter` is row 2 (the first row) so every later lane is displaced once, and the `Spacer` is row 18 (the last row) so its 9pt displaces nothing. Both rows already existed in `tests/fixtures/reference-gantt.json` and were being discarded by the old code.

  New ADRs: [`docs/adr/0021-structural-rows-and-empty-scene.md`](adr/0021-structural-rows-and-empty-scene.md) and [`docs/adr/0022-splitter-band-builder.md`](adr/0022-splitter-band-builder.md). New tests: `tests/GanttCreator.Core.Tests/Scene/SplitterBuilderTests.cs`. **Deferred, recorded not ignored:** the panel/source-row mapping (panel rows are still derived from lane placements), per-row `PanelCellGrid`, `ApplyZOrder` ownership preflight, the read-only protection policy, `StackIndex` visibility, and catalogue write atomicity. The external brief is gitignored as an input document, not a tracked source of requirements.



- **Second review pass over R4.1–R4.5 adapters: 12 findings fixed, 2 declined (this change)** — a second review pass over the landed adapter work. **Two findings were declined, both for the same reason: they assume a COM ownership helper that does not exist.** "Release the captured `_selection` proxy through the existing tested COM ownership helper" and the same ask for `ApplyText`'s `ParagraphFormat2`/`Font2` name a helper that was never built. Every production adapter documents the opposite policy — it takes **no ownership** of Excel-owned shared roots, never calls `FinalReleaseComObject`, and force-releases nothing — and `docs/02-ARCHITECTURE.md`'s "COM ownership and application state" makes that a stop condition rather than a local fix. Adding one is a decision for the row that owns COM lifetime, so the *local-variable* half of the `ApplyText` finding was applied (it is required by the same "no chained COM property calls" rule) and the *release* half was not.

**Four of the fixed findings were silent-failure paths**, which is the class this project treats as a defect: (1) `ApplyZOrder` resolved each shape *inside* the mutation loop, so a name that did not exist left the earlier shapes already moved — the very partial order the method refuses to produce. It now resolves everything first, then sends each shape to the back in **reverse**: `msoSendToBack` is absolute, so walking the scene's back-to-front list from the front end leaves the final order equal to that list **whatever order the host started in**, where the old send-then-bring-forward pass only worked from one starting order. (2) The ownership filter asked `IsOwnedTag`, which proves *a* tag is present, not that it is *this primitive's* tag — a shape carrying another identifier's perfectly valid tag was treated as owned and could be moved, rewritten, or deleted by a refresh. A new `CarriesOwnershipTagFor` compares against `ForPrimitiveId(primitiveId)`, and `Update`/`Delete`/`ListOwned` all use it. (3) `RestoreSelection` tested `is Excel.Range` and **fell through silently** when false, so a host returning a chart or a shape produced a "successful" restore while the user's cursor stayed where the command left it; it now throws and the existing D3 guard records it. (4) `ExcelPanelGridMeasurement` handled `null` and `DBNull` but not the third encoding — the `Range.RowHeight` reference states a mixed-height range "**might return the height of the first row**", which is a *number* that sailed through both absence checks and would have laid out a grid from a height most of the table does not have. The aggregate is now confirmed against every body row, via two new `internal virtual` seams (the row values are still the code under test).

Also: `Create` no longer issues a per-shape `msoSendToBack` (insertion order already is the scene's order, and `ApplyZOrder` is the single authority); `AddDiamond` places from `request.Geometry.Bounds` — the same member `ApplyGeometry` uses — rather than recomputing the box from points, removing a second site where the milestone box could be derived; `SelectBodyRow` now actually degrades on a refused `Activate()`/`Select()`, which its own comment already claimed, because the row is inserted before this runs and a host failure must not report the insert as failed. The live gate gains a fourth primitive (a `ZLayer.Label` text box) and asserts strictly increasing `ZOrderPosition` across all four, so a reversed or layer-only pass can no longer satisfy it.

**Every new validator shipped with a positive test, and the three highest-value tests were mutation-checked** (each reverted after failing exactly as predicted): a forward z-order pass fails the reverse-order pin; the old format-only ownership check fails the mismatched-tag test; removing the per-row height confirmation fails both new panel-grid tests. The z-order test also had to record *which shape* each command moved — a command sequence alone proves how many operations ran, not which shape each one touched. Docs: the R4.1/R4.2 review entry's declined count corrected from two to one (only one was ever named), and R4.2's `Calculation` ledger row moved from STOP CONDITION/pending to **resolved by ADR-0020 (option a)**, with its technical evidence preserved. Gates: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors (`-warnaserror`); Core 969, Office contract **367** (was 361), AddIn 192, Architecture 84, Raster 41, Office-integration-non-Office 14 — all PASS; Pester 197/197; `check-status` and `check-md-links` PASS; `git diff --check` clean. **`verify-office.ps1` Not run** — the four-shape live gate is written and compiles but is unexecuted, so the new z-order pass rests on contract tests, not the host. **`verify-quick.ps1` did not run clean**: its skill-tree gate fails on a **pre-existing untracked** remediation brief under `docs/` that predates this change and was deliberately left out of the commit; every other step was run individually and passes. Checklist: I, J, K.

- **Review-comment pass on R4.1/R4.2: two real defects fixed, one finding declined as a conflict with a landed decision (this change)** — a review pass over the landed R4.1/R4.2 work found **two genuine defects** and **one finding that contradicts an approved decision** (declined, not silently applied).

Defect 1, `ExcelPanelGridMeasurement`: both `Object`-typed PIA conversions treated only `null` as an absent measurement. A body with **mixed row heights** has no single `RowHeight`, so Excel reports `DBNull.Value` — and `Convert.ToDouble(DBNull.Value)` throws `InvalidCastException` out of a **read-only** adapter into the render command. Both conversions now share one `ToPoints` helper that treats `null` and `DBNull` identically and returns the existing `PanelGridRefusalReason.InvalidMeasurement`. The mutation check is the evidence: reverting the helper reproduced exactly that `InvalidCastException`. Six contract tests plus a live `PanelGridMeasurementIntegrationTests` case (**Not run** — see below) that first proves a uniform-height body measures successfully, then sets two rows to different heights and asserts the typed refusal.

Defect 2, `ExcelShapeWriter.Create`: a name failure left **debris**. Per L18 the name carries the full, ADR-0017-unbounded `PrimitiveId`, and the name is written in `ApplyOwnership` — *after* `AddShape`. An over-long identifier therefore placed a shape on the sheet and only then failed, leaving an **untagged** shape that is invisible to `ListOwned`, so neither R4.7 nor R4.8 could ever find it again. `Create` now refuses over-long identifiers before any host call (`MaxShapeNameLength = 255`, the conservative side of L18's approximate figure), and `ApplyOwnershipOrDiscard` deletes the shape and returns `HostRejected` if the stamp is refused for any other reason. `Update` now also authorises on the **ownership tag** rather than the name, exactly as `Delete` does, so a refresh cannot move or rewrite a user shape that merely shares an identifier. That **changes one landed test's expectation**: `An_update_never_rewrites_the_name_or_the_ownership_tag` now asserts a *refusal* rather than a successful-but-untouched update — strictly stronger than the old "never re-stamp" rule, and what entity-guide §"Ownership, editibility, and refresh" requires ("refresh touches only shapes whose alternative text carries a valid tag"). Both members are still left alone. Five new tests, all mutation-checked.

Declined: the `ApplyZOrder` `msoBringForward` → `msoBringToFront` suggestion. The concern is technically sound, but R4.5 D2 and the entity guide both mandate a single pass with **no** `BringToFront`, and `The_z_order_pass_sends_the_first_shape_to_the_back_then_brings_each_forward_once` positively asserts `DoesNotContain("BringToFront")`. Reversing an approved decision is not a local fix; it needs the decision owner. Also fixed: a missing `#pragma warning restore CA1031` after `Report` that was suppressing for the rest of the file, the R4.1 probe status reconciled to **discharged** across all three locations, R4.2's Governing contracts / Acceptance tests / stop condition / first ledger row moved to the ADR-0020 five-setting contract, and the `// __PART3__` generation artifact removed. The failure-injection theory test now asserts the injected failure actually fires — a silent catch had made it vacuous. Gates: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors (`-warnaserror`); Office contract 361/361, Core 969/969, AddIn 190/190, Architecture 84/84, Raster 41/41; `verify-quick.ps1` PASS; `git diff --check` clean. **`verify-office.ps1` Not run** — the new live mixed-row-height test is written and compiles but is unexecuted, so the `DBNull` behaviour rests on mocked contract tests and code reading, not the host. Checklist: A, I, J, K.

- **R4.4's update path is closed: text was create-only, and R4.7 would have inherited a silent staleness defect (this change)** — reviewing R4.4's downstream impact surfaced a gap this row had shipped: `ApplyText` (string + font + alignment) was called from `AddShape` **only**, so `Update` moved a text box and never re-wrote its content. R4.7 reaches `Update` on every reconcile that finds an existing shape, so a refresh that changed a label would have left the box **in the right place saying the wrong thing** — no exception, no error, nothing to notice until a user read the chart. `Update` now re-applies text-box content, justified on the same ground as geometry: a label's content is owned generated data, a user never authors it, so overwriting it is correct. This is deliberately **not** extended to `Name` or the `AlternativeText` tag, which keep R4.1's guarantee (the name is the reconciliation key; the tag is the ownership proof; re-stamping either would silently "repair" a user-edited shape, which is R9.4's job to report). Four contract tests pin it — content re-applied; name and tag untouched *including a user-edited tag*; a bounds-less text box refused **before** the content path can dereference a missing box; a rectangle update never reaches the text path — and the live gate now updates a label and re-reads the string, width, alignment, and font size **off the host**. The new test was mutation-checked and failed as expected with the content branch disabled. `ApplyText` became an `internal virtual` seam (the `ExcelWorkbookInitialiser` pattern) so the content write is observable without walking the `TextFrame2` COM chain, and the test's `NewShape` helper was made **mutable** so the "never re-stamps the tag" assertion could not pass against a shape whose tag was incapable of changing. **The same staleness class remains open for fill/stroke/pattern, which R4.6 owns;** it is now recorded in `IShapeWritePort.Update`'s remarks and in **R4.7's Step 0**, so R4.7 does not assume an update means "make the shape match the request" and knows to delete-and-recreate for a style-only change. Checklist: E, I, J. Next: R4.6.

- **R4.5 polygons and deterministic z-order: the freeform probe found a SILENT failure mode the guide only suspected (this change)** — the row's Step-0 obligation is discharged, and the answer is more consequential than expected. The guide warned that freeform point semantics "differ from assumed" would be a stop condition; what the three-round live probe on Office `16.0.20326.20158` x64 actually found is worse than a wrong assumption: **the obvious call shape fails silently.** Passing three vertices in ONE `AddNodes` call — the natural reading of the signature — produces a **degenerate shape at the correct position**: `L=100 T=90 W≈0 H≈0`, **no exception**. The `Object`-typed `X2..Y3` parameters are a plausible suspect, but converting them to `Single` changed nothing, which *falsified* that hypothesis rather than confirming it; the cause is the multi-vertex call itself. The working pattern is `BuildFreeform` at the first vertex then **one `AddNodes` per vertex**, supplying only `X1`/`Y1` and passing `Type.Missing` for the optional trailing points. Two further facts fall out: coordinates are **absolute** sheet points, the same convention `AddLine` uses, so one convention now covers every family; and `msoEditingCorner` is chosen over `msoEditingAuto` so auto-smoothing can never round a milestone's tips. **This is why the live gate asserts the tip-to-tip EXTENT and not just the position** — a degenerate diamond sits exactly where a real one would, so a position-only test passes against a zero-size shape. Two defects in my own work were caught and fixed: the gate's first run failed because I mis-derived the expected `Left` (a diamond's bounding box starts at `centre − radius`, not `centre`), and my `tip_to_tip` fixture's points did not form the diamond its own name described. **The tolerance question was reopened and RESOLVED against having a second boundary at all (this change).** Commit `1106b99` introduced a second documented tolerance (1e-3pt for the freeform path) and called the discrepancy "accumulated error"; **both halves of that were wrong**, and the follow-up evidence is in the work item. Measured in EMU at exact spans of 10/20/37.5/100/253 pt, the freeform error is a **fixed +1 EMU** — constant across a 25x size range, so it is one quantisation step and not drift — while a plain **`msoShapeDiamond` at the identical bounding box measures 0.000 EMU at every span**, because Excel derives a diamond's vertices from its box rather than storing absolute vertices. So the limitation was a property of **the API I chose**, not of the host: the first probe asked "does freeform work?" and never asked "is freeform the only way to place a diamond?". **`OfficeShapeKind.Polygon` is now `OfficeShapeKind.Diamond`**, the `AddFreeform` path is **deleted** rather than left as dead code, an in-place diamond update became expressible (it is just Left/Top/Width/Height, where a freeform's read-only vertex collection had made it impossible), and **`GeometryMath.Epsilon` (1e-6) governs every family again** — the live gate now reads `W=20 H=20` exactly and passes at 1e-6. A polygon that is not a symmetric axis-aligned diamond is **refused** with `SceneTranslationRefusalReason.NotADiamond` rather than approximated. This is the fourth time this session a plausible Office assumption was falsified by measurement (after `Shape.Tag`, the `xlAlign*` constants, and the multi-vertex `AddNodes`), and the pattern is worth carrying: a probe that establishes a limitation must also establish that the limitation is **unavoidable**. Live evidence: both diamonds at `L=82 T=102` and `L=102 T=102` with `W=H=20.000078`, and the scene's back-to-front order confirmed by the **host's own** read-only `ZOrderPosition` (`bar=1 marker-a=2 marker-b=3`) rather than by restating what the adapter asked for. **Every primitive family now renders**; only `SceneGroup` is reported in `Deferred`, since grouping is Phase 6 export work. Observed: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office contract **347/347** PASS (was 339); Architecture **84/84** PASS; Core **969/969** PASS; Office integration **42/42** PASS; `verify-quick.ps1` **PASS** (11/11 steps, 239.6 s); `verify-office.ps1` **PASS** (25 forced kills against the 26 ceiling). The live milestone gate now reads **`W=20 H=20` exactly** and passes at the single 1e-6 tolerance. Checklist: C, E, I, J. Next: R4.6.

- **R4.4 text, font, alignment, and overflow: the guide's own D3 mapping table was FALSIFIED and is corrected (this change)** — R4.4's ledger already carried "the Office alignment constant spellings" as *unknown until probed*. Probing the installed `ExcelDna.Interop` 16.0.0 assemblies settled it in the row's disfavour: **`xlAlignLeft`, `xlAlignCenter`, and `xlAlignRight` do not exist** anywhere in the Excel or Office.Core interop assemblies. This is the **same class of error ADR-0019 was raised for** — a plausible member name assumed rather than probed — and it was caught only because the row refused to implement from memory. D3 is corrected to `msoAlignLeft` / `msoAlignCenter` / `msoAlignRight` and the ledger row moves from *unknown* to *fact*. **The row also never decided which text API to use, and the two are not equivalent:** the legacy `Shape.TextFrame.Characters` path returns **`Object`**-typed `Font.Name`/`Size`/`Bold`, while `Shape.TextFrame2.TextRange` gives typed `Font2` (`String`/`Single`/`MsoTriState`) and `ParagraphFormat2.Alignment: MsoParagraphAlignment`. **Decision: the `TextFrame2` path**, because an untyped interop member is a live hazard in this codebase — R4.2's `Application.Calculation` `DISP_E_TYPEMISMATCH` and R4.1's `Shape.Tag` were both consequences of trusting an inferred member shape. `OfficeShapeRequest` gains one additive, default-valued `Alignment` member, so R4.7/R4.8 are unaffected. Word wrap and auto-size are switched **off** deliberately: a self-resizing text box would move away from the bounds the scene resolved, which is exactly the silent re-layout D1 forbids. `MapAlignment`'s default arm **throws** rather than defaulting, so an alignment added to the scene enum fails loudly instead of rendering one nobody chose. **Live evidence, all read back off the real shape:** the token font **is present** — `Aptos` at 9pt rendered and read back as `Aptos`/9, with **no silent substitution**, which is the row R8.2a and D-G4 were waiting for; alignment round-tripped **exactly** (`Left → msoAlignLeft`, `Right → msoAlignRight`); and a label the scene had already truncated to `…phase one…` arrived **byte-for-byte**, proving neither renderer nor host re-truncates. The alignment assertion was mutation-checked and **caught** a forced Left→Right swap (`Expected: msoAlignLeft / Actual: msoAlignRight`). **Honest limit on the font evidence:** the reference host happens to ship `Aptos`, so D-G4's *missing-font* case is **untested here, not satisfied** — the gate reports a substitution in output rather than silently accepting it. Observed: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office contract **334/334** PASS (was 323, +11); Architecture **84/84** PASS; Office integration **41/41** PASS; `verify-quick.ps1` **PASS** (11/11 steps, 234.3 s); `verify-office.ps1` **PASS** (25 forced kills against the 26 ceiling — the R4.3 L19 remedy holds). Checklist: E, J, I. Next: R4.5.

- **R4.3 shape render conversion: the origin-translation Step-0 probe is ANSWERED, and it is not the answer the rule assumed (this change)** — a disposable live probe on Office `16.0.20326.20158` x64 established that a **negative shape `Left`/`Top` is silently clamped to zero** — not rejected, not re-anchored, and with **no exception** — and that a zero offset anchors to the **sheet origin, cell `$A$1`**, not the active cell. This is the host evidence entity guide §1's *provisional* zero-origin rule was waiting on, and it makes the rule **load-bearing rather than cosmetic**: because the clamp raises nothing, a renderer that passed scene coordinates through would produce a visibly wrong chart with no error anywhere to catch it. The translation is therefore enforced **structurally** — a new `ChartOriginDelta` type plus `SceneShapeRenderer`, which converts a `GanttScene` into the `OfficeShapeRequest` sequence the R4.1 port already consumes. **That renderer did not exist before this change**: R4.1 landed the port and the `ExcelShapeWriter` rect/line/textbox paths, but nothing translated primitives into requests, which is why the Tier-A scope line "the rect/line rendering half of the shape renderer" is really the row's first delivery of one. `Deferred` reports any primitive family this row cannot draw (text is R4.4, polygons R4.5) **by name**, so a missing shape is never indistinguishable from a correct render. Lines keep the scene's own endpoint direction at creation, because `AddLine` takes absolute `Single` endpoints; the min/absolute-extent normalisation stays on the in-place **update** path, where `Shape` exposes only `Left/Top/Width/Height` and direction is unrecoverable. D4's no-table-read guarantee is asserted **structurally** — the renderer's only constructor parameter is the delta and it holds no reader, table, or worksheet field — because an observed absence cannot fail a test when the offending dependency is added later. **A vacuity defect was found in this row's own live gate and fixed:** the first `ShapeRenderIntegrationTests` derived its expected geometry from `SceneShapeRenderer`'s output, which is self-consistent by construction — mutation moved expectation and observation together and the test still passed (`bar expected L=40 / bar observed L=40`, PASS). The gate now asserts hand-written literals (scene constant + 12pt padding) and re-mutation fails it with `bar Left: expected 52 but the live host reported 40; difference 12 exceeds the documented tolerance 1E-06`. Observed live geometry is exact: `bar L=52 T=42` and `line L=52 T=32 W=180 H=0`, both within `GeometryMath.Epsilon` of the hand-written values. **The Required Office gate is red on the COM-proxy ratchet only, and the prescribed L19 remedy brought it back under the ceiling:** the first run measured **27 forced kills against the 26 ceiling** with all 40 tests passing, so `SuppressLeakSignal` was set on the two tests that create real shapes — this row's new gate and R4.1's `ShapeOwnershipTagIntegrationTests`, which L19 names explicitly — and the re-run measured **25**, giving `verify-office: PASS`. **The ceiling was not raised again**; a second raise would mean the signal has stopped detecting regressions at all. L19's downward direction (26 → 24 → 0) remains **open** and is not claimed as restored. Observed: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office contract **323/323** PASS (was 312, +11); Architecture **84/84** PASS; Office integration **40/40** PASS; `verify-quick.ps1` **PASS** (11/11 steps, 233.3 s); `verify-office.ps1` **PASS**. Checklist: E, J, I. Next: R4.4.

- **ADR-0020: the application-state scope covers five settings, not six (this commit)** — the product owner chose option (a) on the L17 escalation, so calculation mode is **removed** rather than worked around. `Application.Calculation` raises `DISP_E_TYPEMISMATCH` on every access on both the `Application` coclass and the `_Application` interface, confirmed over two full Office-gate runs; the member is correctly typed, read/write, with a virtual setter, so it is COM marshalling on `O365HomePremRetail` 16.0.20326.20158 rather than a call-shape error, and it is one of **19** enum-typed members on `_Application`, so a late-bound `InvokeMember` workaround would have been a spike with a wider blast radius. The decisive fact is that **R4.9's render step writes shapes and never cells** (R4.9 D1), so a calculation-mode change has no consumer in the first live slice — the setting was dropped because nothing needed it, not because it is unimportant. [ADR-0020](adr/0020-application-state-scope-five-settings.md) removes `GanttCalculationMode`, `SetCalculationMode`, its two test seams, and the interop mapping helpers rather than leaving dead code that reads as a supported contract, and amends `docs/02-ARCHITECTURE.md` and `AGENTS.md` so the always-loaded five-setting contract matches the code. **A future command that writes cells must reinstate it with a probed path**, and L17 is now `_closed_` with the host defect itself left explicitly unchanged. Two test changes fell out and are worth naming: the obsolete "undefined calculation mode is refused" test was **removed rather than left passing vacuously**, and the restore-failure test now asserts the injected member stays unrestored (which is stronger than the assertion it replaced) while the other four restore — the D2 guarantee. L17's option (c) was rejected on a correctness ground, not a cost one: a substitute such as `Application.Calculate` cannot *read* the current mode, so D1 capture-on-change would be impossible for that property. Observed: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office contract 310/310 PASS (was 312, minus the removed mode test and its obsolete guard test). Office gate: `Not run` this commit — the scope's five settings are unchanged by ADR-0020, so the last green 39/39 result still stands; it is re-run if the ratchet work below changes production code. Checklist: I, J, K.

- **R4.1's live `AlternativeText` probe is discharged: the tag round-trips unaltered (commit `1a8a823`)** — R4.1's last outstanding Step-0 obligation, and the one it deliberately left open because only a real Excel host could answer it. `ShapeOwnershipTagIntegrationTests.The_ownership_tag_round_trips_through_a_live_shape_unaltered` writes a real ADR-0019 tag through the real `ExcelShapeWriter` and reads it back **off the live shape**: `expected tag = GanttCreator.Owned.v1:83E1472B3D64EBBE`, `actual tag = GanttCreator.Owned.v1:83E1472B3D64EBBE` — byte-identical at **38 characters** (22-char prefix + 16-char hash). **No truncation, so the pre-agreed narrower-hash remedy is not needed and ADR-0019's bounded tag stands as designed.** The test uses a 20-contributor shared-owner identifier (134 chars, the ADR-0017 case) and additionally asserts the tag stays 38 chars for an identifier ten times longer, so a future change that embedded the identifier in the tag would fail rather than pass quietly. **The probe also found a second, separate limit, recorded as L18:** Excel rejects a shape **name** beyond roughly 255 characters with `ArgumentException: The specified value is out of range.` That is a *name* limit — the name carries the full, unbounded shared-owner identifier — not a tag limit, so the ownership filter is unaffected, but R4.7 reconciles on the name and a very large deduplicated entity could exceed the cap. L18 is assigned to R4.7, which is the row that owns reconciliation. **Honest status of the gate: the 39 Office tests all PASS, but `verify-office.ps1` is still red on the COM-proxy ratchet — 25 forced kills against a ceiling of 24.** The probe test contributes the overage: a workbook holding a live shape keeps the process alive, so the fixture escalates to a kill. Three attempts were made and are recorded rather than hidden: (1) wrapping the test's proxies in `OfficeFixture.ComScope` moved the count 26 → 26, proving the leak is the *production* adapter's proxy chain rather than the test body; (2) closing the workbook in the test also left it at 26; (3) deleting the shape and letting the fixture close the workbook brought it to **25**, one over. Attempt (2) additionally caused `RPC_E_DISCONNECTED` on a later run, because closing a workbook disconnects the very proxies the `ComScope` releases afterwards — so that cleanup was reverted. The remaining one-kill overage is a genuine, still-unresolved signal and is **not** papered over: the correct fix is a proxy-ownership change in the shape-write path, which is a decision for the row that owns COM lifetime, not a tweak to a test. The probe's *result* is unambiguous and is what R4.1 needed. Observed: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office contract 312/312 PASS; Office integration 39/39 PASS; `verify-office.ps1` **red on the ratchet only** (25 > 24), with every test passing. Checklist: J.

- **R4.2's application-state scope landed, and the Required Office gate found a real host defect (commit `e704f4d`)** — `ExcelApplicationStateScope` saves and restores `ScreenUpdating`, `EnableEvents`, `DisplayAlerts`, the status bar (both `DisplayStatusBar` and `StatusBar`), the calculation mode, and the selection, with `internal virtual` seams over every read and write. D1 capture-on-change is load-bearing and pinned: `An_unchanged_setting_is_never_written_on_the_way_in_or_out` starts every setting in its post-command state, drives all six suppressions, disposes, and asserts **zero** writes across all six. D2 is pinned by a six-row theory injecting a failure at each property in turn, plus a restore that throws and still leaves the other four restored. D3 is pinned by selection-restored-last, no-restore-without-capture, and a failing-selection test. **The Required Office gate passed** — `pwsh ./scripts/verify-office.ps1` → `verify-office: PASS`, 38/38, against Office `16.0.20326.20158` (`O365HomePremRetail`, x64) on Windows build 26200, first Phase-4 Office evidence row as the roadmap requires. **The gate also found a genuine host defect that no metadata probe could have found, and this row cannot close the six-property contract as written.** `Application.Calculation` raises `COMException 0x80020005 (DISP_E_TYPEMISMATCH)` on **every** access, on both the `Application` coclass and the `_Application` interface, confirmed by two full gate runs. The member is correctly typed `XlCalculation`, read/write, with a virtual setter and three defined values, so this is a COM-marshalling defect on this Office build, not a call-shape error. `Calculation` is one of **19** enum-typed members on `_Application` (`ReferenceStyle`, `WindowState`, `DefaultSaveFormat`, `AutomationSecurity`, `Cursor`, `CutCopyMode`, …), so a late-bound `InvokeMember` workaround is a spike with a wider blast radius, not a local fix. Recorded as **L17 in `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md`** and escalated for a product decision rather than silently dropping a property the architecture contract names; the stop condition ("a seventh property appears → stop; the six-property list is the architecture contract") makes changing the list an architecture amendment, not a local choice. **The live test now asserts the five working settings and does not touch `Calculation`**, so the gate is green and the defect stays visible rather than being papered over. Two further live findings, both recorded: `Application.Selection` is `null` with no open workbook, so the test now calls `fixture.CreateWorkbook()` first; and **`StatusBar` does not round-trip its representation** — an empty status bar reads as the bool `False` but reads back as the string `"FALSE"` after a write and restore, so the comparison is case-insensitive and the equivalence is documented rather than the test failing on a representation change the scope did not cause. Two defects in my own code were caught by tests before commit: the argument guard on `SetCalculationMode` sat **after** the availability check, so an undefined mode was silently ignored when no host existed; and `CaptureSelection`'s guard was inverted, which would have re-captured the post-command selection and "restored" wherever the command left it. Observed: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office contract 312/312 PASS (was 295, +17); Architecture 84/84 PASS; Office integration 38/38 PASS with the forced-kill count falling from 30 to exactly the 24 ratchet ceiling. Checklist: G, J, K.

- **R4.1's two ports landed, and the AddIn's last interop leak is closed (commit `6c18c02`)** — `IShapeWritePort` (create/update/delete by stable scene ID, `ListOwned`, relative z-order) and `IPanelGridMeasurementPort` (measures live column widths and row height into Core's `PanelCellGrid`) now exist beside the other fourteen ports, with `ExcelShapeWriter` and `ExcelPanelGridMeasurement` as their live adapters and `internal virtual` seams over every COM indexer. **`ShapeOwnershipTag` implements ADR-0019**: the shape `Name` is the full primitive ID and the shape `AlternativeText` is the bounded `GanttCreator.Owned.v1:{hash}`, so a caller *cannot* write an untagged shape through the port — R4.8's preservation guarantee is structural, not conventional. Four decisions forced by a probe or a compiler rather than chosen: **`Shape.ZOrder` is a method and `ZOrderPosition` is read-only**, so the port exposes front/back application (anchor the back shape with `msoSendToBack`, then one `msoBringForward` per shape), never index assignment; **`ListColumn` has no `Width`**, so measurement reads `ListColumn.Range.Width`, the `Object` the PIA actually exposes; **a freeform polygon is recognised and refused** rather than implemented, because R4.5's point semantics are still unprobed and a silent success would place a shape on an assumption; and **an in-place polygon update returns false** because Excel's freeform vertices are read-only, leaving R4.7 the delete-and-recreate fallback. **A pre-existing CS0433 violation was found and fixed, not deferred.** R4.1's acceptance criterion "AddIn names no interop types" had no test behind it — `CoreBoundaryTests` only scans `GanttCreator.Core` — so the new `PortHygieneTests.AddIn_sources_name_no_office_interop_types` immediately failed on `src/GanttCreator.AddIn/AddRowCommand.cs`, which has imported `Microsoft.Office.Interop.Excel` since R2.8 and held `Excel.Workbook`/`Sheets`/`ListObject`/`ListRow`/`Range` proxies in the AddIn. `ExcelInsertedRowSelector` and its `IInsertedRowSelector` port moved into `GanttCreator.Office` beside every other COM adapter (Office opens its internals to `GanttCreator.AddIn.Tests` so the existing contract test keeps proving the same behaviour, unchanged at 190/190). **`src/GanttCreator.AddIn` now contains zero references to `Microsoft.Office.Interop*` or `Microsoft.Office.Core`.** `ProtectionGuardFirstTests` gained the four probed shape-creation mutation patterns, so the writer is discovered as mutating and must consult the guard first — a first draft that hid the guard inside a `ResolveShapes` helper failed that check and was corrected to inline the call in all four entry methods, matching `ExcelWorkbookInitialiser`. Three defects in the new architecture test itself were caught and fixed before commit: it reflected Excel's *own* interfaces as duplicate ports; it flagged `IConfigCatalogueReader.Read()` against `IGanttTableReader.Read()` and three `Repair()` methods returning *different* outcome records (the return type is now part of the signature key); and its positive control compared one interface with itself instead of two different ones. Observed: Release build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office contract 295/295 PASS (was 270, +25); Architecture 84/84 PASS (was 78, +6); AddIn 190/190 PASS unchanged after the move. Office gate: `Not run` — R4.1's row is `None`. The live `AlternativeText` truncation probe is R4.1's remaining obligation and is **not** discharged by this commit. Checklist: G, J.

- **ADR-0019: the Excel shape ownership tag rides `AlternativeText`, not `Tag` (this commit)** — R4.1's Step-0 PIA probe, run 2026-09-27 against the installed `Microsoft.Office.Interop.Excel.dll` (`ExcelDna.Interop` 16.0.0) and cross-checked against Microsoft Learn, **falsified a load-bearing assumption that four guides had inherited as fact**: `Microsoft.Office.Interop.Excel.Shape` has **no `Tag` member**. Enumerating all 67 of its properties returns no `Tag`, and the MS Learn Excel `Shape` property list agrees. `Tag` is a *PowerPoint* `Shape` member — R4.1 D2 had conflated the two object models. Because the same carrier is referenced by R4.3 D2 (the reconciliation pair), R4.8 D1 (the ownership filter), and R9.4 (the damage classes), this was an `AGENTS.md` stop condition, not a local fix. [ADR-0019](adr/0019-shape-ownership-carrier.md) resolves it: **`AlternativeText` is the carrier** (probed read/write `String`; `Name` is reserved for the scene primitive ID, `Title` belongs to R9.5 accessibility labels, and `OnAction` is a macro-name field that would create a macro-security surface), and the tag is **bounded and versioned** as `GanttCreator.Owned.v1:{hash}`. Bounded is load-bearing, not cosmetic: under ADR-0017 a shared owner's `SceneOwnerId.Value` is a `|`-joined row list, so its `PrimitiveId` is unbounded, and a truncated tag would break R4.7's reconciliation key and R4.8's prefix filter simultaneously. The tag constant lives in `GanttCreator.Office`, never Core, preserving R3.16's boundary. **The entity guide's `GanttCreator.` prefix requirement is unchanged — only the member carrying it changed.** Four guides amended (R4.1, R4.3, R4.8, R9.4) plus the entity guide and the decisions log. **The same probe also answered two of the manifest's four deliberately-unprobed questions:** R4.2's per-property interop shapes (all six readable; status bar is **two** members, `DisplayStatusBar` `Boolean` RW and `StatusBar` `Object` RW; and `Application.Selection` is `Object` and **canWrite = false**, so selection restore is `Range.Select()` — which makes D3's best-effort-last a mechanical necessity rather than defensive polish) and R4.3's line question (`Shapes.AddLine` takes absolute `Single` `BeginX`/`BeginY`/`EndX`/`EndY`, so exact endpoints *are* expressible; R4.3 D3 now decides the **plain line** over the connector, because a connector's re-routing behaviour would let Excel move a rendered endpoint away from the scene's resolved point and break "renderers consume resolved primitives"). **Two questions remain genuinely unprobed** and are not guessed: the freeform point semantics (R4.5) and the pattern/alpha members (R4.6). **One new obligation lands on R4.1:** confirm live that `AlternativeText` does not truncate the tag; the pre-agreed remedy is a narrower hash, and truncation aggressive enough to force a structural change is a stop-and-report. Also recorded: the guide's "nine Office ports" is **fourteen** (the nine omitted the R2.8/R2.10 ports), and `Shape.ZOrder` is a `ZOrder(MsoZOrderCmd)` method with a read-only `ZOrderPosition`, so the port exposes front/back application, never index assignment. No code changed in this commit — documentation and ADRs only. Office gate: `Not run` — no production behaviour changed. Checklist: I.

- **D-G15: the negative chart origin is answered, provisionally (this branch)** — R3.15 left one question open — whether a negative-origin chart is a defect — and recorded it as `unknown`. The product owner has now decided, and the guide is at **revision 7** with a new §1 subsection "Origin and outer padding". **Two rules:** a renderer **translates the chart bounds to a zero origin and carries the delta**, and **the outer padding stays inside the bounds on all four sides** so that delta must also be carried into export bounds. **Both were already true of the code**, which is why this changes no geometry: `FrameBandsBuilder` has always expanded the content union by `ChartOuterPaddingPt` on all four sides, and `FrameBandsBuilderTests.Builds_exact_bounds_and_chart_owned_primitive_layers` has always asserted the resulting `RectD(-6, 36, 412, 170)`. The decision adds the *placement* rule and makes an existing behaviour explicit. **A correction to my own earlier reporting:** I previously told the user the negative origin was "structurally unreachable in tests" because the golden's `ChartBounds` is `0,6,1120,394` and the fixture union is exactly 1120pt wide, which forces `p = 0`. That was true of the **golden only** and wrong as a general claim — `FrameBandsBuilderTests` already builds with the real default padding of 6 and already pins a negative origin. The negative case was never untested; it was just not in the golden. **Two tests added**, each observed non-vacuous by breaking the behaviour: `FrameBandsBuilderTests.The_outer_padding_is_inside_the_chart_bounds_on_all_four_sides` checks the four padding edges against the content and title rectangles the builder independently reports, and `SceneBuilderTests.The_outer_padding_is_included_and_translating_the_chart_to_a_zero_origin_changes_no_relationship` translates a real scene to a zero origin and proves the translation is lossless across all four primitive kinds and leaves the validator's verdict unchanged. The first failed when the padding was dropped from `X` and `Width` in the builder; the second failed when one polygon point's `Y` was left untranslated — the mutation that proves the per-kind sweep is doing work. **Both mutations had to be re-observed:** the first round was invalid because `Copy-Item` restores a file with its *original* timestamp, so MSBuild skipped recompiling and the suite ran against the mutated `GanttCreator.Core.dll` while `git diff` showed a clean tree. Content was correct and the artefact was not, which is the same trap `PreserveNewest` sets for the copied guide. Every mutation and revert now touches the file to force a rebuild, and a green baseline is established before mutating; the diagnostic message names the offending primitive (`ScenePolygon G-...:marker: top 81 did not shift by the delta`), so a failure is attributable rather than merely observed. **Provisional is load-bearing:** the *contract* is decided and tested in Core, but how the host handles a negative offset is a named **Step-0 probe obligation in R4.3** with its own `unknown until probed` ledger row, because the rule is what the host must end up doing, not evidence that it does. **R6.1 D1** now requires the delta in export bounds, since §1 says empty whitespace outside the bounds is not exported and the padding sits inside them. D-G15 is recorded in the manifest register with the rejected alternatives — anchoring to `PlotBounds` would reintroduce a second source of truth of the kind R3.15 removed, and refusing a negative origin would reject the catalogue's own default padding for the ordinary panel-at-origin layout. Observed: `pwsh ./scripts/verify.ps1` -> **PASS in 255.1s** on the clean tree; non-Office suite **1562/1562** — Core 969 (967 before, 2 new), Office.ContractTests 270, AddIn 190, Architecture 78, Raster 41, untagged integration 14; coverage 100% line and 100% branch for Core, Raster, AddIn, and Office; check-md-links OK; check-status OK; skill tree in sync; `git diff --check` clean. No production code, token, or geometry changed. Office gate: **Not run**. Checklist: C, E, I.
- **R3.16 steps 3-4 — the thirteen-row catalogue and the guide drift guard (this branch)** — `ab132bd` `452e48d` `4e64445`. **R3.15 needed no work: it had already landed** in `e3efd4e` with its dedicated golden commit `d8acd0d`, so this branch is R3.16 only. **Two guide rows were wrong and were corrected first (`ab132bd`, product-owner approved).** The data-panel row named a single `ZLayer.Frame`, but `PanelBuilder` emits **three** layers — body cell fills at `Background` (0), body cell text at `Label` (70), and only the header cells, header text, and shared borders at `Frame` (80) — so a field test transcribed from the uncorrected cell would have failed on every body cell. The procurement row also did not record that `HatchPattern` resolves to `None` for every reference style, so **no committed primitive carries a hatch**; that is now stated as unexercised in the row and in a governing note. Both corrections bring the table into line with the code: no token, z-layer value, label position, or scene member changed, and the golden is byte-identical. **A field now names the member it is read from (`452e48d`).** That is what makes the panel row expressible at all: it spans five primitive families, and a single-primitive row could not have carried a three-layer correction. The catalogue split into `EquivalenceFields.cs` (records), `EquivalenceFieldChecks.cs` (probes), and `EquivalenceFieldTable.cs` (the thirteen rows), and the rows carry the **owner-kind axis** R3.16 called for — the golden holds 24 chart-owned, 30 row-owned, and one shared-`Rows`-owned `a2|a3:delineator`, none of which the old row-only catalogue could assert. Eleven rows are asserted and two are **exclusion rows** that name the token appearing in no identifier, so "a renderer must not invent one" is asserted rather than merely written. The panel row needed a panel-bearing build, since the canonical reference scene omits the optional panel theme and contains no panel primitive; `BuildSceneWithPanel()` adds only the panel, and the committed golden stays panel-free. **Seven new field-stripped positives, each observed non-vacuous** by weakening its probe and watching that test fail: the `CriticalLinePt` overlay height, the delineator's full plot height, the corrected body-cell z-layer, R3.17's parent-rectangle naming rule, R3.15's chart-bounds equality, row ownership, and the delineator label's absent lane/stack keys. **One exemption is recorded rather than faked:** the procurement hatch field is unfalsifiable by construction, because every styled primitive requires a non-null `SceneStyle`; its citation says so and points at the snapshot tests where the hatch contract is actually observable. Manufacturing a positive there would repeat the exact defect the R3.14 review pass found in the milestone draw-order probe. **The drift guard (`4e64445`) parses the table, not prose:** it anchors on the `### Per-entity field contract` heading and the table's own header cell, then consumes only markdown pipe rows, and reports that it cannot find the table if either anchor moves — which is what the work item's stop condition demands. It compares **row names only**; field lists stay in prose. `docs/07-GANTT-ENTITY-GUIDE.md` is now copied to the test output by the csproj, narrowly, for the same reason the fixture and golden are. **Non-vacuity observed against the real guide:** renaming the field-contract Delineator row failed the comparison with that exact name in the message, and the reverted guide was confirmed clean; an earlier attempt that renamed the *representation* table's row instead did not fire, which is correct — the guard reads the table it is meant to read. The catalogue's own citation guard caught **four citations** missing their "Entity guide" prefix and **three duplicate field names** on first run, both fixed. No renderer, no Office path, no production code touched. `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` -> PASS in 236.2s (11 steps, exit 0). `pwsh ./scripts/verify.ps1` -> **PASS in 241.5s** on the clean tree, including PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, actionlint, `dotnet format`, the Release `-warnaserror` build, the packed XLL publish, the package vulnerability scan, and the coverage threshold check: **GanttCreator.Core, Raster, AddIn, and Office all measured 100% line and 100% branch**. Heading, introduction, and the Tier A split are now 94 / 41 / 53 (Tier B 53 was already right); the Tier A bullet's "eight R2 hardening rows" was also off by one and is now nine. **(4) Two Phase-4 guides claimed work that has not landed.** `R4.10` Step 0 opened "R4.1–R4.9 landed" and `R4.9` opened "R4.1–R4.8 landed"; the planned R4.9 command source and its test file (RefreshSheetCommand.cs and RefreshSheetCommandTests.cs) do not exist yet (`Test-Path` false for both) and R4.1 is the next row.
