Office integration gate `Not run` — no Office-hosted behaviour changed and the touched rows carry an Office gate of "None". `verify-quick.ps1`/`verify.ps1` require a committed tree and are reported after this commit. Checklist: B, C, G, I.

- **R3.6-R3.9 branch-end gates (previous commit)** — All four Phase 3 rows are landed (R3.6 `5d49f9c`, R3.7 `e518ca6`, R3.8 `31b72ac`, R3.9 `6b733a4`, plus `eb00c71`). Branch-end `verify.ps1` **PASS in 235.3s** and `verify-quick.ps1` **PASS in 215.8s** on the clean tree. One defect the branch gate caught that the per-commit Release build did not: two `out var` declarations on `TryGetValue` in `CriticalOverlayBuilder` and `LaneEventLayout` violate IDE0008, which `verify-quick`'s production-only format step rejects but `dotnet build /warnaserror` does not surface. Fixed in `eb00c71` rather than folded into a new row commit, because the alternative was a R3.8/R3.9 commit that is not self-greening. **Recorded as a tooling gotcha:** on this repository a green `dotnet build -c Release /warnaserror` is *not* sufficient evidence that `verify-quick.ps1` will pass; the format gate runs `dotnet format` on `src/` only and applies analyzer rules the compiler gate omits. Run `verify-quick.ps1` per commit, not only at branch end. Office integration gate `Not run` for every row — all four carry an Office gate of "None", and no Phase 3 row is host-tested. Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I. Next: R3.10 (delineator lines and labels), then R3.11 and R3.12 to complete Phase 3, then the Phase-3 exit obligation of upgrading the Phase 4 Tier-B guides to Tier A.

- **R3.9 multiple events on one lane (previous commit)** — Added Core `LaneEventLayout` and its tests, the §9 per-lane grouping feed that binds each event to the visual slot R3.4 already derived, so the span, milestone, and critical builders all receive one authoritative slot centre. It introduces **no new geometry rules**: slot centres, the effective stack assignment, and lane growth all come from `LaneGeometry`, and the visible `StackIndex` cell is never read (ADR-0012), which a test pins by feeding wildly different visible values and asserting consecutive Core-derived slots. Two- and three-event stacks are pinned with exact `StackGapPt` separation, mixed bar/diamond slot heights, and lane growth to 34pt for three stacked events rather than compressing them. Same-slot sequential pairs share one centre with no warning; same-slot overlapping pairs share one centre and produce exactly one ambiguity warning, with neither event moved, shortened, or hidden (§9). The §13 planned/actual case is pinned both ways: distinct effective stacks separate vertically with both events at full length, and equal stacks deliberately share the line with both still placed. **Step 0 reconciliation, recorded here as the guide's Notes ledger requires:** `LaneLayoutBuilder.AddAmbiguityWarning` (landed in R3.4) is the authoritative emitter of `AmbiguousStackOverlap`, and R3.9 D2 also claims it, so this feed deliberately re-emits nothing and instead passes the layout's warnings through — otherwise a user would see the same ambiguity twice. Confirmed non-vacuous: temporarily duplicating the warning list fails `Warns_once_for_same_slot_events_that_overlap_and_keeps_one_centre` with a 2-item collection, which proves the single-warning assertion bites. Guards refuse, with positive tests, null input, a missing layout, a null event, duplicate event identity, and a negative effective slot. Three of my own test expectations were arithmetically wrong on the first run (day-to-point offsets and slot centres); each was corrected against the actual geometry rather than by loosening the assertion. Observed: Core 710/710 PASS; Release solution build 0 warnings/0 errors. Office integration gate `Not run` — the row's Office gate is None. Checklist: B, C, G, I. Next: R3.10 (delineator lines and labels), then the Phase 3 exit obligations.

- **R3.8 critical child interval overlay (previous commit)** — Added Core `CriticalOverlayBuilder` and its tests. Entity guide §16 is implemented literally: the overlay is the intersection of the child's inclusive span with the parent's **visible, post-plot-clip** span, sitting on the parent's top edge at `CriticalLinePt` thickness so the stroke stays inside the body; layer 50; role-derived `<rowId>:critical` id. Step 0's risk — misreading "visible parent span" as pre- versus post-plot-clip — is closed structurally: the builder accepts a `IReadOnlyDictionary<GanttRowId, RectD>` of parent **visible** bounds, and a test drives a real R3.6 `SpanBarResult.VisibleBounds` through to prove a plot-clipped parent constrains the overlay to what is on screen. Disjoint, adjacent (shared edge, no gap, no double line, under the inclusive span rule), and overlapping children are each pinned, and a child wider than its parent clips on both sides with exactly one warning while a child wholly outside emits no overlay and one warning. Defence in depth: an orphan or an unresolvable parent is refused with a typed `UnresolvedParent` outcome rather than throwing, which R2.5 already prevents at validation. A note on one Step-0 discovery: a Critical Interval is `EntityKind.Span`, not a distinct kind, so the builder checks the type identity rather than the kind — the first attempt used the kind and did not compile. Observed: Core 693/693 PASS; Release solution build 0 warnings/0 errors. Office integration gate `Not run` — the row's Office gate is None. Checklist: B, C, G, I. Next: R3.9.

- **R3.7 milestone marker and label layout (previous commit)** — Added Core `MilestoneMarkerBuilder` and its tests. Entity guide §20 is implemented literally: a four-point `ScenePolygon` (not a rotated square) centred on `PointEventX(Start)` and the slot-band centre, with `MilestoneSizePt` as the tip-to-tip extent on **both** axes so the bounds are exact across renderers; layer 60; role-derived `<rowId>:marker` id. Only `Start` is read — a fixture that populates `Finish`, `LaneId`, and `StackIndex` asserts the marker does not move, which is the §20/checklist-C rule that milestones never take an extra day. Both inclusive plot edges are pinned (first date at exactly `PlotLeftPt`, last date at exactly its day X) and both out-of-range directions emit no marker plus one warning. The builder refuses, with typed outcomes and positive tests, a null request, a null time scale, a non-finite or zero size (which would collapse the diamond), a milestone with no start date, and a **non-milestone** type — the last is what stops a span from being rendered as a diamond. A non-milestone refusal is the defence-in-depth the guide asks for; R2.5's `NotUsedByType` warning remains the user-facing signal. Observed: Core 674/674 PASS; Release solution build 0 warnings/0 errors. Office integration gate `Not run` — the row's Office gate is None. Checklist: B, C, G, I. Next: R3.8.

- **R3.6 span bar and label layout (previous commit)** — Added the `ITextMetrics` port (width **and** height), `TextMeasurement`, the pinned `FakeTextMetrics`, the shared `LabelText` truncation helper, `SpanBarBuilder`, and `LabelPlanner`, implementing the ADR-0015 contract that landed in the preceding commit. Step 0 confirmed two things that shaped the code: R3.5's `ITextWidthMeasurer` measured width only, so it is now a width-only *view* over the single architecture-mandated seam (`TextWidthMeasurer`/`WidthOnlyMetrics` adapt both ways and R3.5's private `Ellipsize` now delegates to `LabelText`, so the title and description labels truncate by one implementation); and `TimeScale.DateToX` throws for an out-of-range date while `TryDateToX` returns `x = 0` as its failure sentinel, so every clip path branches on the boolean and never inspects the coordinate. Four defects the tests caught, each fixed: a wholly-outside span gained a phantom one-day width because the out-of-range finish edge was unconditionally advanced by `DayWidth`; the cascade accepted a candidate by truncating it, so the ADR-0015 widest-gap fallback could never run and `Inside` was truncated into the body contrary to §22; the `Left` candidate stored its near edge as `X` and then extended by the free width, so the box was anchored ~2x the gap from the shape and fell outside the chart bounds; and `Placed` re-derived the truncation flag by re-testing the *untruncated* text against a box the fallback had already sized, silently reporting no truncation. Confirmed non-vacuous: reverting `_spanAutoOrder` to the superseded `Left → Inside → Right` fails 3 tests, including the ADR-0015 assertion that a fitting `Left` beats a fitting `Inside`. Observed: Core 653/653 PASS; Architecture 78/78 PASS (Core still Office-free); Release solution build 0 warnings/0 errors. Office integration gate `Not run` — the row's Office gate is None. `verify-quick.ps1` `Not run` — it refuses a dirty docs tree, so it runs after this commit. Checklist: A, B, C, E, G, I. Next: R3.7.

- **ADR-0015 — label `Auto` cascade and widest-gap truncation fallback (previous commit)** — Product-owner contract change landed ahead of R3.6, because the entity guide's `fact` rows and §22's undefined "entity's defined fallback" would otherwise have been contradicted by the code. Span `Auto` becomes `Right → Left → Inside` (§12:458, product decision — external sides ahead of the body); the delay event's `Inside` is restated as a *style-level default* evaluated once and never re-entered, escaping to `Right` then `Left` (§17), which the existing `GanttCatalogues` `DelayEvent` preset default already expresses, so **no catalogue column, no ADR-0007 hash change, and no schema-version advance**; §22 gains the widest-gap truncation fallback — free space is the distance from the shape edge to the nearest obstruction, where obstructions are in-lane shapes, placed higher-priority labels, and `PlotBounds` alike — truncated with a single-character `…` and one warning, ties broken by the `Auto` order, and label suppression when no gap holds even an ellipsis. Milestone `Auto`, the §22 placement-priority list, and every token value are unchanged. All four R3.6 guide sites that pinned the old order were corrected (lines 32, D4, the test step, and the `fact` ledger row), plus its status line and two new ledger rows; `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` gained decision **D-G9**; `02-ARCHITECTURE.md` gained a traceability line stating the scene — not the renderer — resolves the label side; the entity guide is now revision 4 and the roadmap revision 7. Verified the old order survives only inside ADR-0015 as historical context and a rejected alternative. Observed: `check-md-links.ps1` OK across 155 files (it first caught a wrong `../adr/` path in the architecture link, fixed); `git diff --check` clean. No production code, so Office gate not applicable. Checklist: I. Next: R3.6.

- **check-status tracked-path hardening + STATUS fix (current branch)** — CI's `check-status.ps1` step failed on the `docs/STATUS.md` claim of the StageInspect audit document, a file listed in `.gitignore` (line 27) and never committed. The gate validated path claims with `Test-Path` only, so the file existed on the maintainer's disk and every local gate (pre-commit, `verify-quick.ps1`) passed, while a clean CI checkout never had it — a local/CI divergence no local filesystem test can reveal. The false reference was introduced by commit `dd5935b` and predates this branch's work. Two changes: the audit-remediation entry now names the audit as a local-only, git-ignored working document and points at the tracked work item `docs/work-items/AUDIT-REMEDIATION-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`; and the gate's path check now also requires `git ls-files --error-unmatch` to know the path, so an on-disk-but-untracked claim fails locally too. Two tests added: the untracked-path rejection, and a positive control that a committed path still passes; the pre-existing "clean status" test now commits its fixture file to match the tightened contract. The new rule immediately caught this entry's own first draft, which quoted the ignored path in backticks — the first live proof that the gap is closed. Confirmed non-vacuous: with the tracking check removed, the new test fails (13 passed / 1 failed). Reproduced the CI failure in a fresh `git clone` before fixing and confirmed the gate passing in one after. Observed: `check-status.Tests.ps1` 14/14; `test-scripts.ps1` 185/185; `verify-quick.ps1` PASS 11/11. Office integration gate `Not run`; `verify.ps1` `Not run` (clean committed tree required). Checklist: G, I.

- **Review-finding fixes, fourth pass (current branch)** — Verified four reported findings against current code; all four were still valid and were fixed. (1) `ValidationReportComposer.IsGeneratedSectionMarker` located the closing `':` with `text.IndexOf("':", index)` over the whole remaining text, so a closing marker on a *later* line satisfied the check and an incomplete header spanning lines was treated as add-in owned, truncating the user's note at the sentinel; the search is now bounded to the header's own line (first `\r`/`\n` after the column quote), so a wrapped header is left untouched. Two `IsOwnedByAddIn_rejects_an_incomplete_marker` cases were added (no closing marker on the header line; closing marker only on a following line) and confirmed to fail against the pre-fix source. (2) `OfficeFixture.ReadStartTimeUtcTicks` caught only `ArgumentException`, `InvalidOperationException`, and `NotSupportedException` from `Process.StartTime`, which also throws `Win32Exception` when the process exits or the OS refuses the handle — the sibling `KillOwnedProcess` already caught that type; the read is split into an ID overload and a resolved-`Process` overload that returns zero for the unverifiable case, matching the sweep's skip-rather-than-kill direction. (3) `OfficeFixture.KillOwnedProcess` called `IsOwnedProcessIdentity(processId)`, which performed its own `GetProcessById`, and then resolved the process a *second* time to kill it, so a PID recycled between the two lookups meant the verified process was not the terminated one; the method now resolves the process once and checks identity against that same `Process`, via a new `IsOwnedProcessIdentity(int, Process)` overload, with the ID-only form kept for the test seam through a `TryGetProcess` helper. (4) The kill-refusal regression test drove `KillOwnedProcess` against the test host itself, so it passed even with the identity guard removed (the runtime refuses to kill the calling process) and pinned nothing; it now launches a disposable `cmd.exe /c ping` child, asserts the call returns false, and additionally asserts the child is still running. Confirmed non-vacuous: with the guard line removed the child is killed and the test fails. Observed: Release solution build 0 warnings/0 errors; Office Contract 36/36 (`ValidationReportComposerTests`, was 33 with the two new theory cases); Office Integration non-Office subset 14/14; production `dotnet format --verify-no-changes` clean. Office integration gate `Not run` — requires a real Excel host and packed XLL. `verify-quick.ps1` PASS; `verify.ps1` `Not run` — requires a clean committed tree. Checklist: B, C, G, I.

- **Review-finding fixes, third pass (current branch)** — Verified six reported findings against current code; all six were still valid and were fixed. (1) The plan's Phase 1 line read "refuse ambiguous duplicate-parent repairs", which could be read as the repair failing; `ExcelGanttRowIdentityRepairer` actually leaves an ambiguous ParentId unchanged while the row Id repairs still proceed and `GanttRowValidator` reports `ParentAmbiguous`, so the plan now states that. (2) `verify-office.Tests.ps1` asserted `Should -Not -Match "Get-Content -LiteralPath \$ownedPidsPath -Raw \| ConvertFrom-Json"` in a double-quoted string, where the backslash is not a PowerShell escape character, so `$ownedPidsPath` expanded to empty and the pattern could never match the forbidden command — the guard was vacuous; it is now single-quoted and a positive control proves the pattern fires. (3) `ExcelInsertedRowSelector.SelectBodyRow` re-read `Application.ActiveWorkbook` after `inserter.Insert` had written, so a cell-change or workbook-activate handler could move the selection to a different workbook; the workbook is now captured in the constructor, which the `RunForExcel` path builds before the write. (4) `GanttScene.TryCreate` rejected only unresolved group children and `SceneGroup` only a direct self-reference, so a cycle spanning two or more groups passed because every child ID resolved; `SceneSnapshot.Deserialize` delegates to `TryCreate`, so the `SceneGroup` claim that "the snapshot layer rejects wider cycles" was false and there was no wider check at all. Added an iterative, stack-safe coloured-walk cycle check returning a new `CyclicGroupChild` refusal, ordered after the unresolved-child check so a cycle cannot be masked as a missing reference, and corrected the comment. (5) `ValidationReportComposer.FindOwnedSectionStart` treated a line boundary plus the bare sentinel as ownership, so a user note whose own line began "Gantt Creator validation:" had its text deleted by `RemoveOwnedSections`; the complete generated-section marker (` row N, column 'F':`) is now required, and the scan continues past a non-matching prefix so a genuine section below a user line is still stripped. (6) The default `OfficeFixture.KillOwnedProcess` killed by PID alone, so a recycled PID could terminate a process the fixture never started; it now verifies the recorded start time still matches, refusing a mismatch and an unverifiable zero. The check sits in the default method, the only code that can touch a real process, so the existing seam-based teardown tests are unchanged. Two existing tests pinned the old bare-prefix contract (`IsOwnedByAddIn_recognises_only_the_sentinel_prefix`, and two reporter fixtures using `WithOwnedNote("stale")` / `" old section"`); they now use complete markers, which is the intended behaviour change from finding 5. Observed: solution build 0 warnings/0 errors; Core 613/613, Office Contract 263/263, AddIn 198/198, Architecture 78/78, Raster 41/41, Office Integration non-Office subset 14/14 (1207 total, 0 failed); `verify-office.Tests.ps1` 27/27 Pester PASS; `verify-quick.ps1` PASS in 196.1s; production format and `git diff --check` clean. Office integration gate `Not run` — requires a real Excel host and packed XLL; `verify.ps1` `Not run` — requires a clean committed tree. Checklist: B, C, G, I, J.

- **Review-finding fixes, second pass (current branch)** — Verified eight reported findings against current code; six were still valid and were fixed, two were skipped. Fixed: (1) `ExcelConfigCatalogueWriter.Write` called `_protectionGuard.Query()`, which reports the *active* sheet, and refused the write when it was protected even though the writer mutates only `_GanttCreatorConfig` — a user who protects their visible data sheet could not regenerate catalogues; `QueryTarget(config)` is the authoritative check and its existing refusal handling is unchanged, and a new `Write_proceeds_when_only_the_active_sheet_is_protected` covers the path (the shared-guard theory now verifies `Query` is never called). (2) `IWorksheetProtectionGuard.QueryTarget` had a default implementation delegating to `Query()`, so an implementation that did not override it silently substituted the active sheet for the target and could authorise a refused mutation; it is now abstract (`ExcelWorksheetProtectionGuard` already implemented it, and the one hand-written test fake now implements it), and the doc claim that the adapter "validates it as an Excel worksheet belonging to the active workbook" was removed — no membership check exists. (3) `ExcelConfigCatalogueReader.IsOptionalUppercaseColour` used `ColourHex.TryParse`, which accepts `#RRGGBBAA` and surrounding whitespace, while `GanttStyleDefinition.ValidateFormatting` uses `GanttColourToken.IsValidHex`; a user style row carrying either form passed the reader and then threw `ArgumentException` from the registry constructor instead of returning a typed refusal — both now use `GanttColourToken.IsValidHex`, with three new `Read_refuses_a_user_style_with_a_malformed_resolved_colour` cases confirmed to fail against the pre-fix source. (4) `verify-office.ps1` ran both `taskkill` calls in `Remove-HarnessOwnedOfficeProcesses` without discarding stdout, so taskkill's success text was emitted into the function's output and `return $killed` no longer yielded only the count; `| Out-Null` was added at both call sites, matching `publish-addin.ps1`, with stderr redirection and exit-code handling preserved. (5) `ExcelInsertedRowSelectorTests.Activates_the_worksheet_before_selecting` asserted the two call counts separately, which passes even with the order reversed; activation and selection now share one recorded sequence that is asserted as `["activate", "select:2"]`. (6) The R2.7c roadmap gate required a save/reopen contract round-trip that no test provides, contradicting the work item's D4 (`Office gate: None`; R2.7b already carries the live save/reopen evidence) — the gate now names the registry-rebuild round-trip that does exist, and the manifest records R2.7c as `Implemented` with the 90-guide/27-Tier-A/63-Tier-B counts. Skipped as already valid: `GanttRowValidator.CheckCriticalParents` order dependence, already fixed by commit `92d2ab0` (`PropagateBlockedParents` repeating to a fixed point, with `Critical_interval_child_of_an_ambiguous_interval_is_blocked_in_either_input_order` asserting identical issues in both orders); and the manifest count target of 89, since the index actually holds 90 guide rows (27 Tier A + 63 Tier B) and 89 would have left new drift. Office gate `Not run` — the writer's target-protection change and the reader's colour rule alter live COM behaviour and `verify-office.ps1` is the gate that must consume them. `verify-quick.ps1` PASS 11/11 (exit 0); `git diff --check` clean. `verify.ps1` `Not run` — it requires a clean committed tree. Checklist: C, D, G, I, J.

- **Truncation surrogate test** — `Truncate_marks_a_clipped_note_and_never_splits_a_surrogate_pair` now seeds its `StringBuilder` with one ASCII character and appends two-unit characters until the text *exceeds* `MaxNoteLength`, so the clamp point lands on a high surrogate and the guard in `ValidationReportComposer.Truncate` must fire. The length assertion changed from exactly `MaxNoteLength` to `MaxNoteLength - 1`, which is what actually proves the orphaned high surrogate was dropped before the `...` marker was appended; the old even-length input never exercised the branch, so the test passed whether or not the guard existed. Production code unchanged. Observed: Office contract 250/250 PASS; `verify-quick.ps1` PASS in 190.8s; `git diff --check` clean. Office integration gate `Not run` — no production behaviour changed. `verify.ps1` `Not run` — requires a clean committed tree. Checklist: C, D.

- **Review-finding fixes (current branch)** — Verified each reported finding against current code; all five were still valid and were fixed. `GanttRowValidator.CheckCriticalParents` decided a Critical Interval against a parent row that had not been decided yet, so a child authored above its parent survived as an event while the same rows reversed did not; a new `PropagateBlockedParents` pass blocks every still-undecided Critical Interval whose canonical parent is blocked, repeating to a fixed point so chains are covered and the outcome no longer depends on input order. `ExcelTypeOptionsMaterialiser` gained a repair-only `MaterialiseForRepair` entry point (the interface default) so `ExcelConfigRepairer` can replace a TypeOptions name left pointing at the previous catalogue range — ordinary `Materialise` keeps the `NameTargetInvalid` refusal — and a real `EnsureCurrent` that returns success without writing when the stored name and the Type-column validation are already current, instead of the interface default that re-deleted and re-added the same validation on every Add Row; the shared guard call stays in each public entry point so `ProtectionGuardFirstTests` still holds, and an `internal virtual GetRefersTo` seam substitutes the COM parameterised `Range.Address` read the expression tree cannot mock. The representative acceptance fixture gave its overlapping planned/as-built pair the same stack index, and the `StackIndex == 1` assertion was satisfied by the unrelated "Foundation pour" span, so the pair is now stacked 0/1, "Foundation pour" moved to stack 2, and the test asserts the two rows' own stack indices plus the overlap precondition. `ExcelInsertedRowSelectorTests` lost its dead `Sheets[1]`/`ListObjects[1]` Moq setups (`TestableSelector` overrides both accessors). `OfficeFixture` replaced an `HRESULT == 0x80010108` catch — `RPC_E_CALL_REJECTED`, not an "already closed" signal, so it silently swallowed a genuine Excel-busy error — with explicit `MarkWorkbookClosed` caller tracking; the caller-closed test now marks its workbook. Observed: Debug solution build 0 warnings/0 errors; Core 609/609 PASS; Office contract 250/250 PASS; AddIn 198/198 PASS; Architecture 78/78 PASS; Raster 41/41 PASS; non-Office 1176/1176 PASS; `dotnet format --verify-no-changes --exclude tests` PASS; `git diff --check` PASS (the CRLF warning on `RepresentativeAcceptanceFixtureTests.cs` is pre-existing: the file is CRLF at HEAD). The new validator regression test was confirmed to fail against the pre-fix source (row 2 got no `ParentInvalid`). Office integration gate `Not run` — the `OfficeFixture` teardown change and the new COM read in `EnsureCurrent` are exercised only by `verify-office.ps1`. `verify-quick.ps1`/`verify.ps1` `Not run` — clean-tree/packed-XLL gates, not attempted in this session. Checklist: C, D, J.

- **Office harness process-leak remediation (commits `751d33b`, `0d0d44a`, `b9a006d`)** — The Office gate leaked `EXCEL.EXE` processes that held the packed XLL, so the **next** run failed in the ExcelDnaPack publish step before any sweep could run. Five fixture-side defects and three shell-side defects fed it, all now fixed and covered. Fixture: the owned-PID manifest was read with a whole-file `ConvertFrom-Json`, which throws on the one-record-per-line file and left the ownership set empty (so the "primary ownership signal" silently degraded to parentage); a manifest record carried only a PID, and Windows recycles PIDs, so a stale manifest could name an unrelated process — each record now carries `StartTimeUtcTicks`; PID capture recorded a process only when exactly one new EXCEL appeared, so zero-or-several left it at 0 and disabled both the orphan poll and the handoff; `PollForProcessExitAsync` returned void and teardown ignored it, so a survivor was tolerated — it now returns a result, escalates to a targeted kill of the fixture's own PID, and reports a survivor as a cleanup failure. Shell: the sweep ran only on the timeout path, so a green run left its strays behind — it now runs **before the build** (preflight, from the previous run's manifest) and on the PASS/FAIL paths, and a killed stray **fails the gate** (exit 3) rather than warning; ownership requires PID **and** matching start time, with unverifiable records skipped (fail-safe); `publish-addin.ps1` carries the same preflight for `verify-quick.ps1`/`verify.ps1`, which publish the same XLL. The packed-XLL gate dropped from five cycles to one: its wait is its whole cost (tens of seconds per cycle) and proves the same open-side fact each time, five-cycle no-orphan is already covered by `Excel_open_close_five_times_no_orphan`, and reopen/session re-arm by the D2 contract test — close-record attribution remains an open R1.6 item. Work item C (releasing the test-held COM proxies) is now **measured rather than invisible**: the gate reports `COM proxy leak signal`, which is **25 forced kills across 37 tests**, target 0, and `OfficeFixture.ComScope` is the conversion mechanism. Observed: `verify-office.ps1` **PASS 37/37** with **0 surviving EXCEL.EXE** (was 5 strays); non-Office **1179/1179** PASS; `verify-office.Tests.ps1` **23/23** PASS; production format, status accuracy, and `git diff --check` PASS. Checklist: C, D, J.

- **TypeOptions name-target normalisation fix (commit `4902da3`)** — The live-Office gate failed 3/34 `AddRowIntegrationTests` with `TypeOptionsUnavailable`. The cause was **pre-existing**, reproduced identically on the parent commit `69a986d` and therefore not introduced by the review-finding work: `ExcelTypeOptionsMaterialiser` wrote the defined name as `='_GanttCreatorConfig'!$B$2:$B$17` but Excel normalises a stored name and echoed it back unquoted as `=_GanttCreatorConfig!$B$2:$B$17`. The `StringComparison.Ordinal` guard at the name-target check compared the two raw strings, so it rejected the adapter's own correct value on **every** call and refused every Add Row, deleting the row it had just inserted. The guard now compares the sheet name (unquoted, unescaped, case-insensitive — Excel's own sheet-name semantics) and the range address (Ordinal) separately via `RefersToMatches`/`ParseRefersTo`, so Excel's normalisation is accepted while a name pointing at a different sheet or a different range is still refused. A temporary diagnostic spike (`TempDiagnosticSpike`) captured the stored and built `RefersTo` strings and was deleted after use. Added 10 positive contract cases covering Excel's unquoted echo, the quoted form, case-insensitive sheet match, an escaped-quote sheet name, a different range, a different sheet, an unparsable target, and a null stored target. Observed: Office contract 243/243 PASS (was 233); live `AddRowIntegrationTests` 4/4 PASS (was 1/4); all non-Office 1172/1172 PASS. Checklist: C, D, J.

- **StageInspect review findings (commit `3726a4f`)** — Verified each reported finding against current code and fixed six, skipped one. Fixed: `ExcelInsertedRowSelector` now takes the table-relative `ListRows[bodyIndex]` instead of indexing `table.Range.Rows` by an absolute worksheet row (correct only when the table starts at row 1) and activates the Gantt worksheet before `Range.Select`, which otherwise fails on an inactive sheet; `CheckCriticalParentCycles` stops at an edge whose target Id is duplicated, so an ambiguous parent is reported as `ParentAmbiguous` instead of a cycle the data does not support; `GanttStyleChange.ChangeStyleKey` returns the row unchanged when the style key is unchanged, so re-picking the current style no longer discards the user's per-row fill/stroke/label overrides; `WorkbookStateSubscription.Dispose` is re-enterable, retains a delegate whose removal failed, and retries it on a later dispose while callbacks stay disabled after the first; the identity repairer no longer rewrites a blank `ParentId` cell or a `ParentId` whose target Id is duplicated (ADR-0011 leaves those for `ParentAmbiguous`) — the blank and duplicate guards moved out of a loop that performed no writes into the loop that does. Skipped as already valid: the `GanttStyleResolution` registry claim, which commit `69a986d` (R2.7c) fixed; `TryResolve` already prefers the registry, the Type default, then the built-in preset, and `Resolver_reads_a_user_style_from_the_registry` proves a Custom Activity style outside the presets resolves. Added a positive regression test per fix, each confirmed to fail against the pre-fix source (the validator test's pre-fix output reported `ParentCycle` where `ParentAmbiguous` is correct). Observed: Debug solution build 0 warnings/0 errors; Core 608/608 PASS; Office contract 233/233 PASS; AddIn 198/198 PASS; Architecture 78/78 PASS; Raster 41/41 PASS; all non-Office 1162/1162 PASS; `verify-quick.ps1` PASS 11/11 (exit 0); production format, status accuracy, and `git diff --check` PASS. Office gate `Not run` — the AddIn selection and the identity-repair write path change live COM behaviour and the tagged `AddRowIntegrationTests`/`ConfigRepairIntegrationTests` are the gate that must consume them. Checklist: C, D, J.

- **R2.7c — Style registry carries resolved formatting (current branch)** — `GanttStyleDefinition` now carries each named style's resolved formatting and default label position (optional, so the capability-only path stays legal), with construction-time validation in `GanttStyleRegistry` and `HasFormatting` so an unformattable style refuses rather than rendering blank. `GanttStyleResolver.TryResolve` gained a registry-taking overload that prefers a registered style, then the Type default, then the built-in preset, so a user-authored style in `tblGanttStyles` is finally resolvable while the pre-existing overload is unchanged. `ExcelConfigCatalogueReader` projects every already-validated user-style resolved column, and now validates the numeric and colour columns it previously trusted — a user row with a negative metric, a lowercase colour, or a non-numeric metric was being projected as `0`/empty and would have rendered as a silent blank style. - **Audit remediation (StageInspect code audit) — 7 phases** — Worked the StageInspect code audit (a local-only working document, git-ignored alongside the other findings/plan files) through an approved work item, `docs/work-items/AUDIT-REMEDIATION-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`, with every implemented, rejected, or deferred proposal recorded. (1) Target-aware protection via `IWorksheetProtectionGuard.QueryTarget`, wired into every mutating Office adapter; identity repair plans before writing and remaps unambiguous `ParentId`s; plot-anchor repair resolves `tblGanttData` by table name and uses the live column count. (2) Exact `tblGanttConfig` key validation, duplicate-preservation refusal (`CataloguePreservationInvalid`), TypeOptions name-target validation, operation-based repair counts, and distinct visibility/anchor/identity refusal reasons. (3) `GanttStyleResolver` (named-style base plus per-row overrides, Type-default fallback, typed refusal) and `GanttStyleChange.ChangeStyleKey`; `ITypeOptionsMaterialiser.EnsureCurrent()`. (4) Line-anchored validation-note ownership (a user's mid-sentence mention is no longer truncated), line-oriented notes, marked Unicode-safe truncation, and an AddIn-layer `IInsertedRowSelector` so Add Row lands the selection on the new row. (5) `SceneGroup` refuses duplicate children and self-reference, `ScenePrimitive` refuses negative `sortOrder`, `RectD` refuses edge overflow while allowing negative coordinates. (6) Removed the unused `ExcelDna.Interop` package from Raster and the unused Raster project reference from AddIn (both verified against source usage first), and added per-handler `EventHandlerState` so a failed detach is recorded `DetachFailed` rather than reported as success. (7) Added `RepresentativeAcceptanceFixtureTests`, a 10-scenario fixture covering overlaps, lanes, critical parentage, milestones, delineators, overrides, and a hidden row. Contract conflict found and resolved by human decision: the first identity implementation refused ambiguous duplicate-parent repair, contradicting accepted ADR-0011; ADR-0011 was preserved, the refusal was removed, and `GanttValidationCodes.ParentAmbiguous` now reports those rows through normal validation. - **R2.7a risk closure — guard-first enforcement, live refusal evidence, L15/L16 (commit `4ffc3ad`)** — Closes the risks recorded against `4c6c648` with enforced behaviour rather than notes. New `tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests/ProtectionGuardFirstTests.cs` (12 tests: 4 rules + 8 positive controls) classifies every `src/GanttCreator.Office/*.cs` file, fails on an unregistered data-mutating adapter, requires a protection read before the first data mutation in each registered adapter and inside its entry method, and requires `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` L16 to name every adapter that has not adopted the guard port — so R2.8 cannot add a mutating adapter without the guard. Observed RED against the real sources: a `.Value2` write prepended to `Initialise` produced "mutates workbook content before its first protection read" (file restored byte-identical, SHA-256 `C7CADF…35A`), and a temporary extra Office source file (RedProbe.cs) containing `ListRows.Add()` produced "mutates workbook content but is not registered as a mutating adapter" (probe deleted). `tests/GanttCreator.Office.IntegrationTests/InitialiseSheetIntegrationTests.cs` gains two live refusal tests (real `Worksheet.Protect()` → guard `SheetProtected`; Initialise → typed `TargetProtected`, both zero-mutation, 5/5 PASS live), which also exercises the `_Worksheet` seam against real COM instead of Moq only. `ExcelWorksheetProtectionGuard.cs` loses its `IDE0046` suppression (ternary form, no `NoWarn` change); `WorksheetProtectionGuardTests.cs` uses the concrete local type (CA1859). Ledger: L15 (no-undo consequence of ADR-0008 D2/D3), L16 (guard-port adoption debt, test-enforced). - **R2.2 — Initialise Sheet** (commits `bb4386a`, `b1ffdd8`, `e4bfc34`, `4fe3203`, 2026-09-18) — the initialiser adopts the active blank worksheet as `tblGanttData`'s home when one is available; otherwise it creates the Gantt Data worksheet. A blank visible `Worksheet` that is the active sheet is renamed and populated in place (adopt path); a chart sheet, any non-empty worksheet, or the absence of an active worksheet causes a fresh worksheet to be created (create path). Typed refusals — NoActiveWorkbook, TableExists, ConfigSheetExists, TargetProtected — mutate nothing and surface one concise message per refusal through the presenter; presenter failure degrades. New: `GanttWorkbookContract` (`ConfigSheetName`, `GanttSheetLabel`, `PlotAnchorDefinedName`), `IWorkbookInitialiser` port + `ExcelWorkbookInitialiser` (read-only checks run before any mutation; the create path rolls back a just-added sheet when a protection re-check after creation would block later writes), `WorkbookInitialiseOutcome`/`WorkbookInitialisePath`/`InitialiseRefusalReason`, `InitialiseSheetCommand` (silent success, one actionable message per refusal, presenter failure degrades), `btnInitialiseSheet` in the Data group gated on the workbook fact. The shipped AddIn gate asserted "exactly two gated controls" against in-flight R2.2 wiring — the count literal is `2 → 3` and travels with the new control in this commit, plus a per-control "exactly one getEnabled" guard so a duplicated attribute cannot slip through. - **fix: reused-PID flag in `Test-HarnessProcessTreeActive` was scoped to the last matching process, not the candidate** — in `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1` the `$isReusedPid` exclusion flag was set inside the `foreach ($process in $matching)` loop but read by the parentage-expansion guard *outside* it, so the guard reflected only the last matching process of the previous candidate. Two failure modes: (a) once any candidate set the flag `$true` it was never cleared, so a later unrelated candidate inherited it and its children were skipped from the traversal even though they belong to an exited owned parent that must still be walked; (b) for a candidate PID with multiple live instances (a reused PID) the guard used whichever process happened to be last, not the candidate as a whole. Introduced a per-candidate `$candidateIsReusedPid`, reset to `$false` at the top of each candidate iteration (after the visited-Pid check, before the matching-process loop); the reused-PID branch sets it to `$true` rather than merely `continue`-ing, and the guard reads that per-candidate flag, so descendants of exited owned parents are traversed regardless of what a previous candidate did. New Pester regression test in `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.Tests.ps1` (per-candidate reset position, guard reads per-candidate flag, old loop-scoped `$isReusedPid` no longer the guard's input, reused-PID branch sets the flag). Gates: `Parser::ParseFile` 0 diagnostics on both files; PSSA (Error/Warning) clean on both; `scripts/test-scripts.ps1` 164/164 PASS; `git diff --check` clean. Checklist: G. Uncommitted (working tree on `stage-inspect`); `verify.ps1` not run — it refuses on a dirty tree.

- **fix: PSSA `PSAvoidAssignmentToAutomaticVariable` on `ci-parity.Tests.ps1:278`** — the `Resolve-DeclarationEvents` loop used `$event`, a PowerShell automatic variable, as its iteration local (introduced in `8f9a0ac`, the scalarVars declaration-history refactor). Assigning to an automatic variable clobbers it for the rest of the script's event handlers, so the rule fires at Warning severity and `verify-quick.ps1` step 4 (`Invoke-PssaGate`) exits 1. Renamed the local to `$declEvent` at all 6 sites (278, 279, 280, 288, 289, 311); no behaviour change — the variable is private to that function and unreferenced anywhere else in the repo (verified by `Select-String '$event'` across all `*.ps1`/`*.cs`). Deliberately did **not** add the rule to `PSScriptAnalyzerSettings.psd1` `ExcludeRules`: that would disable a real safety rule to hide one misuse, contradicting the file's own "exclusions are deliberate and documented" contract. Gates: PSSA over `scripts/` with the repo settings (`Invoke-PssaGate -Path scripts -Settings scripts/PSScriptAnalyzerSettings.psd1`) → 0 findings; `scripts/test-scripts.ps1` 161/161 PASS (incl. `ci-parity` 33/33); `git diff --check` clean; diff is 6/6 lines. - **fix: per-step teardown guards in AutoClose (R1.6 review finding)** — `src/GanttCreator.AddIn/AddInHost.cs` `AutoClose` now guards each teardown operation on its own: `RibbonStateService.Reset()`, the close record, `CommandBoundary.Reset()`, `DiagnosticsService.Reset()`, and the log dispose (which keeps its `finally` clearing `_log`/`_sessionToken`). Previously only the close record had its own guard, so a throw from `RibbonStateService.Reset()` skipped the close record *and* both singleton resets, and a throw from `CommandBoundary.Reset()` skipped `DiagnosticsService.Reset()` — contradicting the method's own "every step is individually guarded" comment, work item `docs/work-items/R1.6-deterministic-shutdown.md` D1, and the existing `ExcelApplicationAdapter.Dispose` pattern. Silent degradation and the never-throw contract are unchanged. New tests: `tests/GanttCreator.AddIn.Tests/AddInHostTests.cs` gains `AutoClose_close_record_failure_does_not_skip_the_singleton_resets` (a throwing close-record write still drops the captured boundary's log, clears `DiagnosticsService`'s path, and disposes the log), and the new `tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests/AddInHostTeardownGuardTests.cs` parses `AutoClose` and requires each of the four teardown operations to sit inside exactly one catch-bearing `try` with no shared guard. Its positive controls include the pre-fix shared-guard shape, observed RED against the `HEAD` revision of `AddInHost.cs` (temporary substitution, byte-identical restore verified by file hash) with `LogClose is enclosed by 2 try statement(s)` and `shared guard for: CommandBoundary.Reset, DiagnosticsService.Reset, RibbonStateService.Reset`, plus a deleted-step control. The three `Reset` calls are non-throwing by contract, so their guards are unreachable defence in depth with no runtime failure to inject; that limitation is recorded in the work item. - **fix: W14 scalar-variable scope-chain walk + cast-assignment LHS guard (`scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1`)** — Two still-valid defects in the `Get-DotnetTestProjectTokenCount` tripwire. (1) The `ConvertExpressionAst` branch recorded the operand variable of *any* `[string]` cast, including a bare cast used as a value expression (e.g. `[string]$x` in a command argument); only a cast that is the **Left** of an `AssignmentStatementAst` proves a scalar assignment. Guard added: `if ($parent.Parent -is [AssignmentStatementAst])` before recording the operand. (2) The variable lookup checked only the immediate function scope, so a scalar `[string]$Solution` declared in script scope (or a parent function) was invisible to a command inside a nested function — a legitimate single-target invocation was miscounted as zero. Fix: new `Get-EnclosingScopeChain` returns the ordered chain from innermost function out to `__ScriptScope__`; the lookup walks that chain and counts on the first match, preserving same-named-variable independence (each scope's scalar set is keyed separately). Gates: `scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1` 25/25 PASS (Pester 6.1.0); `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` 12/12 PASS (151.1s); `git diff --check` clean. Checklist: G, I.
- **fix: diagnose-l12 Step 1 deadline kill awaits the owned tree and aborts on non-exit (`scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1`)** — The `WaitForExit(5000)` call after the Step 1 deadline kill previously discarded its bool (`$null = $step1Proc.WaitForExit(5000)`), so a tree that failed to exit within 5s fell through to reading and deleting the redirected streams while the child still held them open (partial reads, silently-failed deletes, leaked temp files). The bool is now captured into `$step1TreeExited`; a `False` result logs a FAIL and exits 3 before any stream access, so the streams are touched only after confirmed process termination. The error/cleanup `finally` block keeps its own discard form, which is why the new Pester test scopes the negative assertion to the deadline branch. Two new Pester tests with a positive control: capture-and-abort ordering (+ failing stub that discards the bool). Gates: `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.Tests.ps1` 27/27 PASS (Pester 6.1.0); PSSA 0 findings on both modified files; both files parse clean; `git diff --check` clean. Checklist: G.
- **fix: W14 scalar-variable tracking keyed by name + lexical scope (`scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1`)** — The `Get-DotnetTestProjectTokenCount` helper keyed scalar `[string]` declarations by variable name alone, so a `[string]$Solution` declared in one function vouched for a same-named collection `$Solution` in a sibling function or at script scope; the tripwire would have counted a collection as one positional target and let a `dotnet test $Projects`-style defect pass. Fix: `scalarVars` is now `Dictionary<string, HashSet<string>>` keyed by scope name; new helpers `Add-ScalarVar` and `Get-EnclosingScopeName` record each declaration against the nearest enclosing `FunctionDefinitionAst` (or `__ScriptScope__` when there is none), and every command variable is resolved against its own enclosing scope before scalar validation. Added a parity-test fixture `same-named variables in different lexical scopes remain independent` asserting counts `(1, 0, 0)` for a function-scoped `[string]$Solution` invocation, a sibling function's same-named collection, and a script-scope same-named collection. Verified the old name-only logic produced `(1, 1, 1)` on the fixture (genuine regression guard). Gates: `scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1` 25/25 PASS (Pester 6.1.0); `git diff --check` clean. - **fix: EnvVarGuardScanner masking coverage for #elif branches and non-DEBUG conjunctions (uncommitted, working tree on `stage-inspect`)** — `tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests/EnvVarGuardScanner.cs` `MaskCommentsAndStrings` now parses the source once per referenced symbol in three new modes while preserving the existing DEBUG-inclusive per-symbol parses: (1) DEBUG-excluded per-symbol parses, toggling each non-DEBUG symbol alone, which activates `#elif` branches whose `#if` is DEBUG (e.g. `#if DEBUG ... #elif FEATURE`); (2) an all-symbols parse with every non-DEBUG symbol defined at once, which activates conjunctions of non-DEBUG symbols such as `#if A && B` or `#elif A && B` under a false preceding `#if` — no single-symbol parse can satisfy these. DEBUG is left undefined in both so `!DEBUG`-gated branches stay reachable, and DEBUG is filtered out of the DEBUG-excluded/all-symbols symbol sets because defining DEBUG without DEBUG is contradictory. The explanatory comment was rewritten to describe the full expanded parse coverage. - **fix: L12 diagnostic review-finding batch 3 (Step 1 owned-process teardown, runtime-aware createdump resolution, dump-written exit/size contract)** — Three verified findings fixed, none skipped. (1) `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1` Step 1: the dotnet-test launch, poll, and classification are now wrapped in `try/finally` around `$step1Proc` (nulled before launch); the finally block terminates the owned testhost process tree with `taskkill /PID $step1Proc.Id /T /F` on every path — normal exit, the existing deadline branch, and any terminating error or interruption after launch. (2) Step 0: the requested runtime version is now read from the standard `runtimeOptions.framework` (single) or `runtimeOptions.frameworks` (multi-framework list) schema instead of the non-standard `Microsoft.NETCore.App.RuntimeVersion` property, and the installed runtime is roll-forward-resolved (latest patch of the requested minor band, else latest patch of the lowest higher minor band within the same major) before locating `createdump.exe`. Framework parsing or version resolution failure now skips Step 2 with a log line rather than degrading to the arbitrary recursive `createdump.exe` selection the step exists to avoid. (3) Step 2: `dump-written` is now reported only when the createdump exit code is 0 AND the dump file exists with size > 0; a failed createdump that leaves a zero-byte or partial artifact, and an exit-0 run that leaves only such an artifact, are now classified distinctly (`other-failure`/`access-denied` note the partial artifact; `no-dump-exit-zero` distinguishes absent vs zero-byte). Gates: `scripts/test-scripts.ps1` 129/129 PASS (13 in the diagnose harness, up from 10); PSSA 0 findings on both modified scripts; a synthetic roll-forward spike covering same-band/latest-patch, lowest-higher-minor-band roll, multi-framework selection, self-contained, unparseable, and no-installed-runtime cases all PASS; `git diff --check` clean (the pre-existing CRLF warning is unchanged from the clean tree). Checklist: G. Uncommitted (working tree on `stage-inspect`).
- **fix: L12 diagnostic review-finding batch (createdump runtime-aware discovery, Step2DeadlineSeconds validation, timeout-branch crash-flag removal, KNOWN-LIMITATIONS L12 rewording, ci-parity --diag option-value consumption, EnvVarGuardScanner per-symbol #elif masking)** — Seven verified findings fixed, none skipped. (1) `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1` Step 0: createdump discovery now resolves the .NET runtime used by the OfficeIntegration testhost (`GanttCreator.Office.IntegrationTests.runtimeconfig.json` → `Microsoft.NETCore.App.RuntimeVersion`) and selects the createdump.exe under that runtime's shared framework directory, with a version-matched recursive fallback; the same resolved `$createdump` path is used and logged by both Step 0 and Step 2. (2) Step 1 pre-flight: `$Step2DeadlineSeconds` validation added alongside the existing `$Step1TimeoutSeconds`/`$Step1DeadlineSeconds` checks — zero or negative values exit 2 through the same validation path before Step 2 can produce a misleading timeout-killed result. (3) Timeout branch: `$step1Crashed = $true` removed; a Step 1 deadline hit reports `outcome=timeout` only, so the summary never conflates timeout with crash. (4) `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` L12 entry: the debug-privilege / antivirus-EDR diagnostic is now explicitly stated as **unimplemented** — the parent-process dump smoke test cannot validate that hypothesis because createdump dumps its own parent process, not an arbitrary remote target. (5) `scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1`: `Get-DotnetTestProjectTokenCount` now consumes the value following `--diag` as an option argument (via a `$skipNext` flag) instead of counting it as a positional target; new positive control `tripwire accepts dotnet test --diag with a .sln-named diagnostic file and no target` expects `@(0)`. (6) `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.Tests.ps1`: the `createdump` assertion tightened to `Start-Process -FilePath $createdump.FullName` so declarations or log messages alone cannot satisfy the check. (7) `tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests/EnvVarGuardScanner.cs`: the referenced-symbol parse now iterates each symbol individually (plus DEBUG) instead of defining all symbols in one parse, so every `#elif` branch becomes active in at least one parse and its literal/comment spans are masked. Value unchanged (still C# 14 / 1400); the named `LanguageVersion.CSharp14` member cannot be used against the installed Roslyn 4.14.0 compile asset (verified: build error CS0117), so the numeric cast is retained. `const` also satisfies CA1802. Gates: build 0 warnings/0 errors; `GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests` 55/55 PASS.
- **fix: L12 diagnostic review-finding batch (ci-parity per-invocation target validation, bare-timeout suffix pattern, Step 1 pre-flight + abort classification, Step 2 smoke-test rewording + stale-dump guard)** — Four verified findings fixed, none skipped. (1) `scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1`: `Get-DotnetTestProjectTokenCount` now returns one target-token count per `dotnet test` invocation (an array) instead of an aggregate total; the W14 guard iterates those counts and reports the offending invocation index. New positive control `tripwire rejects a zero-target invocation alongside a valid one` (expects `@(0, 1)`). (2) `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.Tests.ps1`: the bare-timeout pattern at line 39 now allows the numeric timeout to carry a duration suffix (`60s`) or be followed immediately by whitespace, end-of-input, or a comma; a new positive control covers the `60s` case. (3) `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1`: Step 1 no longer treats every fast `dotnet test` exit as a testhost crash — pre-flight validates the solution path, positive timeout/deadline, and built `GanttCreator.Office.IntegrationTests` artifacts (exit 2 on each); the process is started with redirected stdout/stderr; crash classification captures output and exit status and only sets `$step1Crashed` on a testhost-abort signature, with invalid-argument output routed to a distinct `invalid-args` outcome. (4) Step 2 reclassified as a parent-process dump smoke test (synopsis, header comment, inline comment, and summary all say so; EDR-blocking validation directed to the runtime-triggered path targeting a different process such as `$helperProc`), and the stale dump is removed before each run. New tests `Step 2 never reuses a stale dump from a previous run` and `Step 1 validates inputs and build artifacts before launching`. - **fix: review-finding batch (Roslyn 4.14 pin + scanner language pin, ci-parity bare `dotnet test` guard, L12 createdump diagnostic Step 2 rewrite, STATUS open-questions hygiene)** — Six verified findings fixed, none skipped. (1) `Directory.Packages.props` pins `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` 4.12.0 → 4.14.0 and `tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests/packages.lock.json` is regenerated via `dotnet restore` (resolved 4.14.0, content hash updated); `EnvVarGuardScanner` now passes an explicit C# 14 `LanguageVersion` (numeric `1400`) at every `CSharpParseOptions` construction feeding `CollectMaskSpans`. The named member cannot be used: the installed 4.14.0 package's lib/net9.0 compile asset has no `LanguageVersion.CSharp14`, while its lib/net8.0 asset defines `CSharp14 = 1400` (both verified by reflection on the installed package); the numeric value is accepted by the parser and equals the named member where the member exists. (2) `scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1`: the W14 validation guard and `Get-DotnetTestProjectTokenCount` now accept end-of-line (not only whitespace) after `dotnet test`, so a bare `dotnet test` invocation enters validation and is counted (still flagged by the exactly-one-target contract); a bare-line case was added to the zero-target tripwire as the positive control. (3)+(4) `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.Tests.ps1`: both bare-timeout pattern declarations now allow the numeric timeout to be followed immediately by whitespace or end-of-input (the old pattern required two extra characters, so the positive-control stub `--blame-hang-timeout 600` before a newline could never match and the negative control was silently dead), and the paired-flag assertions now match the script's comma-separated PowerShell argument array — separate `'--blame-hang-dump-type'` and `'none'` arguments, and `'--blame-hang'` required as a standalone argument so longer related flags cannot satisfy the check. (5) `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1`: the Step 1 crash-age classification now measures the process lifetime from its own exit/start timestamps (`ExitTime - StartTime`) instead of the watchdog stopwatch, which included up to the 1 s polling quantum and could misclassify rapid crashes; Step 2 rewritten — each redirect parameter gets one valid path variable (the old `-RedirectStandardOutput $env:TEMP Step2-out.tmp` passed two tokens and aborted `Start-Process` binding before createdump ever ran), the invocation no longer uses `-Wait` but polls the returned process against a new `-Step2DeadlineSeconds 120` deadline, terminates the owned createdump process on expiry and continues to the summary, the dump path is passed via createdump's supported `-f`/`--name` option (no `-o` option exists), the PID argument is dropped because the installed createdump 10.0.12 rejects it (live-verified: "The pid argument is no longer supported", exit -1; per dotnet/runtime createdumpmain.cpp — see src/coreclr/debug/createdump/ in the dotnet/runtime repository — createdump writes a dump of its parent process and a target PID cannot be specified), and the helper teardown moved into a `finally` so terminating failures from the dump probe still execute it before propagating. The new invocation shape was smoke-tested live (`createdump -f <path> -n` with redirected output → exit 0, dump written, empty stderr). (6) `docs/STATUS.md`: the historical R1.2 evidence-asymmetry note moved out of "Open questions for human" (now consistent with its `- _none yet_` state) into the status history at the end of this section, content preserved. Gates: `dotnet test tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests` 55/55 PASS; `scripts/test-scripts.ps1` Pester 118/118 PASS (ci-parity 18/18, diagnose-l12 8/8); PSSA gate clean on all modified scripts; `git diff --check` clean; `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` PASS (see next commit's record). 
- **fix: review-finding sweep (gitignore diagnostic-tooling rules, boundary log wording, scanner directive-comment masking)** — Three verified findings fixed, one skipped. (1) `.gitignore` no longer ignores the L12 createdump diagnostic tool sources (`scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1`, `scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.Tests.ps1`) so both are trackable; the diagnostic-tooling section now ignores only the local L12 diagnostic directory (generated, ignored, and not committed) (also covered by the pre-existing generated-artifact ignore rule; `git check-ignore` verified both scripts unignored and the output directory still ignored). (3) `EnvVarGuardScanner.CollectMaskSpans` now enumerates `root.DescendantTokens(descendIntoTrivia: true)` so a trailing comment on an `#if`/`#elif` directive line (structured trivia) is masked before `DirectiveRegex`/`CollectReferencedPreprocessorSymbols` process it; without it such a comment produced a spurious "malformed preprocessor expression" finding plus cascading false positives. New regression test `Scanner_ignores_call_mention_in_trailing_comment_on_if_directive` in `tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests/DebugHookGuardTests.cs`, observed RED before the fix (malformed-expression + cascading false findings) and GREEN after. Skipped finding: the proposal to loop over project paths in `scripts/test-non-office.ps1` is stale — the current single-solution invocation is the deliberate fix in commit `536d20d` for the `b54ccbd` MSB1008 regression, and the W14 tripwire in `scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1` asserts exactly one target per `dotnet test` line ("hardcoding project lists is what enabled the W14 drift"). Gates: `dotnet test tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests` 55/55 PASS; `dotnet test tests/GanttCreator.AddIn.Tests` 86/86 PASS; `git diff --check` clean; `verify-quick.ps1` "skill tree in sync" step FAILs on the pre-existing uncommitted `docs/STATUS.md` dirty-tree guard (clears on next commit); `verify.ps1` not run — it refuses on a dirty tree.


- **fix: mask literals and comments inside preprocessor regions inactive in both scanner parses (`EnvVarGuardScanner`)** — Review finding verified against the tree and reproduced: a region guarded by a symbol other than `DEBUG` (e.g. `#if FEATURE`) is inactive in both mask parses (no symbols / DEBUG defined), so Roslyn reports its contents as skipped tokens inside structured trivia; `DescendantTokens()` never enumerates them, leaving comments/string literals inside such regions unmasked — a call mention in such a comment produced a false finding and a directive-lookalike inside such a literal produced a spurious "unterminated #if". `MaskCommentsAndStrings` now extracts every symbol referenced by the source's `#if`/`#elif` conditions (new `CollectReferencedPreprocessorSymbols` + `IdentifierRegex`, excluding `defined`/`true`/`false`) and merges a third `CSharpParseOptions` pass with all of those symbols (plus `DEBUG`) defined, so those regions become active and their literal/comment spans are collected and merged. Two new regression tests in `tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests/DebugHookGuardTests.cs` (comment mention and raw-string directive-lookalike inside a doubly-inactive region); both observed RED before the fix and GREEN after. Known residual limitation (documented, unchanged behaviour): a condition mixing defined and undefined non-DEBUG symbols (e.g. `#if FOO && !BAR`) is still inactive in all three parses; full coverage would require 2^n parses. Gates: `dotnet test tests/GanttCreator.Architecture.Tests` 54/54 PASS; `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` PASS in 152.4 s (all steps); `git diff --check` clean. Uncommitted (working tree on `stage-inspect`); `verify.ps1` not run — it refuses on a dirty tree.

New W14 Pester tripwire in `scripts/ci-parity.Tests.ps1` (`dotnet test entry points pass exactly one project or solution`): every `scripts/*.ps1` `dotnet test` invocation must pass exactly one project/solution target token; positive control fires on the b54ccbd multiple-project form and on a zero-target form; negative control accepts the single-solution form; plus a `-Solution` parameter-preservation assertion (verify-quick parity). Local-only hygiene: scripts/diagnose-l12-createdump.ps1 (gitignored/untracked, so invisible to CI and deliberately not backticked here because check-status verifies backticked paths exist in the checkout) carried a non-ASCII em-dash without BOM that failed the local PSSA gate but not CI's; replaced with ASCII. - **feat: add command error boundary with operation IDs and user-safe messages (R1.4)** — One command boundary (`src/GanttCreator.AddIn/CommandBoundary.cs`) for every Ribbon callback. A thrown command produces exactly one rolling-log record (`CommandError: op=GC-<timestamp>-<n> command=<id> stage=ribbon-callback excel-version=<x|unknown> exception=<type> hresult=0x<hex> detail=<redacted>`) and exactly one user-safe dialog (concise category sentence + operation ID + log pointer; never exception text, stack, or paths). The boundary is failure-proof (log-write and dialog failures degrade, never propagate into Excel). Operation IDs are deterministic (injected `TimeProvider` + monotonic counter, invariant culture, UTC). `CommandErrorTranslator` is a pure category map (COM/SEH, permission, I/O, cancellation, generic); `CommandErrorDialog` uses the managed WinForms `TaskDialog` (Error icon) with a MessageBox fallback (ADR-0006 pattern, no P/Invoke). `OnDiagnosticsClick` now routes through the boundary; its local CA1031 catch and the superseded `DiagnosticsService.WriteDiagnosticsError` are removed (one error-writing path). `AddInHost` wires/resets the boundary alongside `DiagnosticsService`. A committed `#if DEBUG` force-failure hook (`DebugForceFailure`, `GANTTCREATOR_FORCE_COMMAND_FAILURE=<commandId>`) is compiled out of Release and pinned by a directive-aware `EnvVarGuardScanner` that masks comments and string/char literals, models the `#if`/`#elif`/`#else`/`#endif` branch stack, and accepts a read only when the conjunction of enclosing conditions is unsatisfiable with `DEBUG` unset (so `DEBUG || FEATURE` is rejected, `DEBUG && FEATURE` accepted); it scans the whole `src/` tree (excluding `bin`/`obj`/generated/Designer sources) so a stray read anywhere is caught. Cancellation wording in `CommandBoundary.Translator` updated. Tests: `CommandBoundaryTests`, `DebugForceFailureTests`, `GanttRibbonTests`, and the rewritten `DebugHookGuardTests` (repo-wide positive control + positive/negative/structure fixtures). Gates: `dotnet build` 0 warnings/0 errors; `dotnet test` AddIn 78/78, Architecture 40/40 PASS; `dotnet format --verify-no-changes --exclude tests` exit 0; `git diff --check` clean.

- **fix: CRLF line-ending hygiene — `core.autocrlf=true` affected only paths without an explicit eol policy** — The repo had `core.autocrlf=true` set at the local level while `.gitattributes` mandates `text eol=lf` for `*.cs` and friends. Autocrlf does not override those explicit eol rules; it affected only the paths whose attributes did not specify an eol policy, namely the previously uncovered `*.resx` files, which were checked out with CRLF in the working tree while the index stored LF: `git diff` (which normalises) showed empty, but `git status` flagged those files as modified. Fixed by setting `core.autocrlf=false` and `core.eol=lf` in the local config (`.gitattributes` is now authoritative going forward) and running `git add --renormalize .`, which re-staged `src/GanttCreator.AddIn/RibbonResources.resx` (67 lines, CRLF→LF). That file was the only one affected because `*.resx` was not covered by any attribute rule, so it had been left in raw CRLF; the rule `*.resx text eol=lf` was added to `.gitattributes` so the gap is closed. Verified: `git ls-files --eol` reports zero `wcr` entries across the whole tree, `git diff --check` is clean, `git status` shows only the staged `.gitattributes` change. Gates: `pwsh ./scripts/pre-commit.ps1` → PASS (skill tree in sync, status accuracy 68 hashes/67 paths/13 roadmap IDs, markdown link sanity 38 files). commit `402f305`.
- **fix: verify-office sweep requires positive parentage, not mere absence from the pre-run snapshot** — `Remove-HarnessOwnedOfficeProcesses` in `scripts/verify-office.ps1` previously killed every `EXCEL`/`POWERPNT` PID that appeared during the run but was absent from the before-snapshot, which could kill a user-owned Office process the user started while the tests were running. It now takes a second `OwnedTreePids` array (the children of the owned `dotnet test` launcher, captured via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` before `taskkill` runs) and kills an Office process only if it is not in the before-snapshot **and** its `ParentProcessId` is in the owned tree; an Office process whose parent cannot be determined is skipped, never killed. `ShouldProcess` confirmation and `taskkill /PID /T /F` by PID are preserved. `.DESCRIPTION` step 4 updated; two new Pester cases (`sweep requires a positive parentage test`, `sweep refuses to kill when the parent cannot be identified`) added to `scripts/verify-office.Tests.ps1`. Gates: `scripts/verify-office.Tests.ps1` 13/13 PASS; PSSA 0 findings on all modified scripts; `git diff --check` clean; **live `pwsh ./scripts/verify-office.ps1 -DeadlineSeconds 600` → 8/8 PASS, exit 0, no orphan, and the pre-existing user-owned EXCEL.EXE (PID 25544, parent `explorer.exe`) was left running — the sweep logged `Skipping Office process PID 25544 (parent 13596 is not in the owned dotnet-test tree)` rather than killing it.** **`L13` in `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` is now closed by the fixture-to-shell PID handoff** (`OfficeFixture` records its owned Excel PID into `$env:GANTTCREATOR_OWNED_PIDS_PATH`, read by the sweep as the primary ownership signal; parentage is only the fallback). Residual parentage-fallback exposure recorded as `L14`. commit `402f305`.

The RibbonX document is stored in `src/GanttCreator.AddIn/RibbonResources.resx` as a `ResXFileRef` to `src/GanttCreator.AddIn/RibbonResources/Ribbon.xml`, read through the committed `src/GanttCreator.AddIn/RibbonResources.Designer.cs` accessor; `src/GanttCreator.AddIn/GanttCreator.AddIn.csproj` declares `NeutralLanguage` and resource generator metadata, and `docs/02-ARCHITECTURE.md` records the embedded-resource standard. 7 contract tests in `tests/GanttCreator.AddIn.Tests/GanttRibbonTests.cs`. - **fix: anchor verify.ps1 dirty-tree check to repo root with git -C** — The 'working tree hygiene' step in `scripts/verify.ps1` used `git status --short` which resolved against the caller's CWD; running verify.ps1 from a subdirectory could report a clean tree while the repo root was dirty. Changed to `git -C (Split-Path -Parent $scriptRoot) status --short` to anchor the check to the repository root regardless of CWD. No contract change — the clean-tree requirement is identical. - **Two-gate sequence and human-gated branch lifecycle documented** — `docs/05-GIT-QUALITY.md` § "Local gates" no longer says "Before every commit/PR: verify.ps1" (a doc/code contradiction that caused the R1.1 session to burn a full pre-commit `verify.ps1` run on a staged tree): the section now records the two-gate contract — `verify-quick.ps1` is the every-commit gate run after staging (accepts staged changes; validates the staged index), `verify.ps1` is the branch-final gate run once after the last commit (clean tree required, fail-fast step 1). § "Branch and review policy" gains the human-gated lifecycle rule: the agent never pushes and never opens a PR unasked; the agent's branch-final self-certification is a green `verify.ps1` after its last commit; the CodeRabbit review stage runs on the pushed branch; the human creates the PR once satisfied. `docs/work-items/TEMPLATE.md` evidence commands re-annotated ("accepts staged changes" / "branch-final, after the LAST commit — requires a clean tree"); `docs/06-LLM-PROTOCOL.md` pre-flight checklist updated so every agent session starts knowing the sequence. Skill views regenerated via `scripts/sync-cline-skills.ps1`. Gates: `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` → PASS (see commit); branch-final `verify.ps1` → PASS on the clean tree (see commit).

- **verify.ps1 hygiene step moved to first** — The 'working tree hygiene' step in `scripts/verify.ps1` now runs as step 1 (was step 14), so a dirty tree fails in ~0.1 s instead of after the ~2-minute suite (cost paid during the R1.1 session when a pre-commit `verify.ps1` run burned the full suite before failing on the dirty-tree check). No contract change: the clean-tree requirement is identical, only its position — `verify.ps1` remains the branch-final gate run after the last commit, while `verify-quick.ps1` stays the per-commit gate that accepts staged changes. `scripts/step-parity.Tests.ps1` (parses `.DESCRIPTION` labels against `Invoke-Step` names at runtime) passes unchanged once the description enumeration matched the new order. Gates observed: step-parity 11/11 PASS; live `verify.ps1` on a dirty tree → step 1 FAIL in 0.1 s (positive demonstration); `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` → blocked by `check-cline-skills` because `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` and `docs/STATUS.md` are intentionally uncommitted.

- **L12 closed: verify-office watchdog replaces --blame-hang-timeout** — `scripts/verify-office.ps1` no longer passes the blame-hang collector; the `$DeadlineSeconds` deadline is enforced by an external watchdog (`Start-Process -PassThru` + `Stopwatch` poll every 5 s; soft timeout kills the owned `dotnet test` process tree with `taskkill /PID /T /F` and re-runs the harness-owned Office-process sweep as a safety net). New `scripts/verify-office.Tests.ps1` (auto-discovered by `test-scripts.ps1`): no-`--blame-hang-timeout` assertion with positive control, plus isolated harness cases (hanging stub `dotnet` → exit 124 + `TIMEOUT`; prompt stub → exit 0 + PASS). `KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` L12 marked `_closed_`; `STATUS.md` Known limitations mirrors. Gates: `scripts/verify-office.ps1` 5/5 PASS (27.8 s, no orphan); `scripts/verify-office.Tests.ps1` 10/10 PASS; PSSA 0 findings on both modified scripts; `verify-quick.ps1` blocked by `check-cline-skills` because `docs/KNOWN-LIMITATIONS.md` and `docs/STATUS.md` are intentionally uncommitted.

- **CA2007 narrowed to affected test code, Office fixture green** — Removed the project-wide `CA2007` entry from `tests/Directory.Build.props` `NoWarn` (now only `CA1707;IDE0011`); the comment records the approved 2026-09-11 exception — test/fixture awaits use `ConfigureAwait(true)`, satisfying both CA2007 and xUnit1030 with no precedence declared — and directs future awaits to file-/member-scoped suppressions with written justification. Scoped the affected code instead: `OfficeFixtureTests.cs` disposal rewritten from `await using` (which CA2007 cannot see a `ConfigureAwait` on) to explicit `try/finally` + `await DisposeAsync().ConfigureAwait(true)`; `OfficeFixture.cs` `PollForProcessExitAsync` await gained `ConfigureAwait(true)`; the two general `catch (Exception)` aggregation points in `DisposeAsync` and the guarded `Quit()` cleanup in `InitializeAsync` carry `#pragma warning disable CA1031` with written justification (first-error aggregation, rethrown via `ExceptionDispatchInfo` / original error preserved). Also removed the never-read `quitAttempted`/`quitSucceeded`/`releaseAttempted` bools (CS0219). STATUS.md history note: the "Office fixture + L3 re-date" entry's "suppressed CA2007 ... conflicting with CA2007's library-code ConfigureAwait(false)" wording is superseded — CA2007 is now enforced in tests, no conflict claimed. Gates: `dotnet build -c Release GanttCreator.slnx` 0 warnings 0 errors; `dotnet build -c Debug` on the Office integration project 0/0; Architecture 19/19; Core + AddIn Release builds 0/0. Office-host proof (2026-09-11, this machine's O365 x64 install, blame collector omitted per L12): `dotnet test -c Release tests/GanttCreator.Office.IntegrationTests` 5/5 PASS (23 s), no orphan — the `try/finally + ConfigureAwait(true)` disposal timing is confirmed equivalent on live Excel, risk retired. `scripts/test-scripts.ps1` / `verify-quick.ps1`: Not run (no `pwsh` on the tool PATH in this session).
- **actionlint pin single-sourced in ci.yml** — The "Workflow lint (actionlint)" step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` hardcoded `$version = '1.7.7'` and a literal SHA-256 (`7f12f180…`), duplicating `scripts/tool-versions.psd1#actionlint` (W11 drift surface). Now the step imports the psd1 and derives both `$version = $versions.actionlint.Version` and `$expectedHash = $versions.actionlint.Sha256` at runtime, and builds the URL from `$versions.actionlint.DownloadUrl -f $version` instead of a hardcoded string. The unused `$expectedHash = $versions.actionlint.Sha256` was also removed from the PSScriptAnalyzer step (it assigned a value nothing in that scope consumed). The two W11 tripwires that asserted a literal mirror of the hash in ci.yml (`ci-parity.Tests.ps1` "actionlint SHA-256 in ci.yml equals…", `lint-ci.Tests.ps1` "ci.yml mirrors the same actionlint pin") were rewritten to assert the import + property reference instead — the same indirection as the existing PSScriptAnalyzer test — since a literal mirror is no longer present to compare. Both tripwires keep their step-scoped extraction so the import in the PSScriptAnalyzer step cannot satisfy them. Gates: `scripts/test-scripts.ps1` 88/88 PASS (Pester 6.1.0); PSSA 0 findings over all three changed files; `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` 12/12 PASS (329.3 s, incl. the actionlint download+SHA-256 step re-verified at runtime); `git diff --check` clean.
- **check-status path predicate extracted to shared helper** — The repo-path shape predicate (separator + extension-like suffix, no whitespace/glob/URL/dash noise) and the wildcard-metacharacter rejection that followed it were inlined twice: once in the validation loop and again as an inline `Where-Object` filter in the `check-status: OK` summary count. Extracted both into one function, `Get-RepoPathTokenStatus`, returning `IsPath`/`HasWildcard`, so the loop and the verified-path count share a single definition. The count now excludes wildcard-rejected tokens (`IsPath -and -not HasWildcard`), so a rejected token can never be reported as verified. Added two positive Pester cases in `scripts/check-status.Tests.ps1`: a path-shaped token carrying `?`/`[]` exits 1 with the documented message, and a non-path token such as `[Fact]` exits 0 (the wildcard rule must not fire on attribute annotations). Gates: `scripts/test-scripts.ps1` 88/88 PASS (Pester 6.1.0); PSSA 0 findings over both changed files; `pwsh ./scripts/check-status.ps1` → `check-status: OK (63 hashes, 58 paths, 10 roadmap IDs verified)` — identical to the pre-refactor baseline; `git diff --check` clean.

- **pre-commit verify-quick source-text assertion hardened** — Replaced the invocation-pattern regex (`(pwsh|\$\(|&)\s?.*verify-quick\.ps1`) in `scripts/pre-commit.Tests.ps1` with a direct filename match after stripping all comments (XML doc block + line comments). The old regex could miss multi-line invocations (`.*` does not cross newlines), multiple-space invocations (`\s?` allows only one), and non-standard invocation patterns. Added two positive-control tests: one verifying the check catches direct, wrapped, and multi-line invocations; another verifying the filename is allowed only in comment/documentation contexts. Gates: `scripts/pre-commit.Tests.ps1` 16/16 PASS (Pester 6.1.0); PSSA 0 findings; skill-tree drift in sync; `git diff --check` clean.

New review-runbook tripwire fails on any sub-minute duration token on a line mentioning a verify gate (review-runbook 7/7). (2) `d01713c`: `scripts/test-locked-restore.ps1` claimed run 2 succeeded "purely from the lock file with no network access" but never denied network — both runs were plain `dotnet restore --locked-mode --no-cache`, which can reach nuget.org unnoticed. It is now a four-run proof: runs 1-2 deterministic from-clean; run 3 negative control (NuGet.config whose only source is `https://127.0.0.1:1`, a closed loopback port, plus `NUGET_PACKAGES` isolated to an empty directory) MUST fail — proving the block denies package acquisition; run 4 offline proof (same blocked source, normal cache) must succeed purely from lock files + local cache. Blocked runs pass `-p:NuGetAudit=false` (NuGetAudit fetches vulnerability data from the source and fails NU1900 Warning As Error under warnings-as-errors even when the lock file alone suffices — observed on SDK 10.0.400; plain-HTTP blocked sources are unusable, NU1302 at config validation). Harness updated: the `dotnet` stub exits non-zero exactly when `NUGET_PACKAGES` is isolated; new assertions cover the four restores, the blocked-configfile argv, and the isolated control. Evidence: real run observed — runs 1-2 PASS, run 3 failed with NU1301 connection refused as required, run 4 PASS offline, script exit 0; Pester 75/75 (`scripts/test-scripts.ps1`); `pwsh ./scripts/verify-quick.ps1` 12/12 PASS (97.3 s, 2026-09-10, pre-`d01713c` state).
